#5111. Elizabeth Warren vows scrutiny of prediction markets, Trump family crypto ventures as Senate Banking chair

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Sen. Warren will be focused on affordability, artificial intelligence, prediction markets, a looming potential financial crisis and accountability, an aide said.

Sen. Elizabeth Warren is setting her sights on prediction market oversight and the Trump family's burgeoning crypto businesses when she becomes chair of the Senate Banking Committee in the new year.

Warren, who has been critical of crypto, will be focused on four main areas when she helms the committee in January, after Democrats took control of the Senate last week, including affordability, artificial intelligence, prediction markets, a looming potential financial crisis, and accountability, a Warren banking committee aide said.

"Warren is looking to focus on the issues that have been the focus of her career and drove the election outcomes across the country on Tuesday - making life more affordable for families across the country, reining in industries that are raising costs and undermining the financial security of Americans, and rooting out corruption," the aide said on Monday.

Warren's plans come after a Democratic blue wave swept the midterm elections last week, shifting leadership in the House and Senate. Congressional committee chairs drive the agenda, so Warren will lead efforts involving banks, deposit insurance, housing, the Federal Reserve – and crypto. The Senate Agriculture Committee and the House Financial Services Committee will both also be key panels for crypto, where newly elected former Sen. Sherrod Brown and longstanding Rep. Maxine Waters are poised to lead, respectively.

Warren has taken an aggressive stance against Trump and his family's crypto interests over the past year. In letters, she has asked for an investigation into Trump's memecoin as well as an ethics probe into alleged conflicts tied to Trump-family-backed crypto project World Liberty Financial, the United Arab Emirates, and Trump aides.

"Big Tech CEOs, Administration officials, Trump family crypto businesses, Wall Street executives, and giant corporations should preserve their documents and prepare to answer tough questions," the aide said.

Warren has also repeatedly criticized prediction market platforms including Kalshi and Polymarket and has asked for answers around election-related bets and how both companies plan to prevent insider trading. Lawmakers have repeatedly brought up concerns about insider trading over the past year that were then ignited after an active-duty U.S. Army soldier was charged for using confidential information to place bets on the prediction market Polymarket ahead of former Venezuelan President Nicolás Maduro’s capture earlier this year.

In another, a former White House teleprompter agreed to pay more than $172,000 to settle charges that he used his advance access to President Trump's speeches to profit on Kalshi.

"She is looking to end the current Wild West-style lack of oversight in this area, with corrupt insiders profiting," the aide said.

#5110. North Korean hackers create memecoins to launder funds from Binance hack: Elliptic

The blockchain firm traced stolen funds through six new tokens on NEAR, where hackers used liquidity pools to obscure transfers between wallets.

North Korean hackers created memecoins to launder cryptocurrency stolen in the $6.3 billion Binance hack, routing roughly $430,000 through six newly launched tokens on the NEAR blockchain, according to a report from blockchain analytics firm Elliptic.

Between Nov. 5 and 7, the hackers created at least six tokens on NEAR and used liquidity pools on decentralized exchanges to move stolen funds between wallets, Elliptic said. The tokens attracted almost no outside trading and were abandoned shortly after launch.

The hackers used one set of wallets to swap stolen NEAR tokens for the new memecoins, then used another set to sell those memecoins back into the pools for NEAR. The swaps let them move funds without direct wallet-to-wallet transfers, though the transactions remained traceable onchain.

How stolen NEAR moved between wallets through liquidity pools. Source: Elliptic

Elliptic also found that five of the six token creators were funded with proceeds from earlier token sales just two to 13 minutes before the next launch, linking the tokens in a chain of transactions.

“North Korea’s launderers rarely rely on a single technique,” Elliptic co-founder Tom Robinson wrote in the report. “Instead, they layer many cheap techniques, each adding a little more delay.”

Elliptic said the same laundering technique was also used following the $1.5 billion Bybit hack in 2025, when North Korean-linked actors moved at least $24 million through a newly created token on the TRON blockchain.

At the time of Elliptic’s analysis, about 11.8 million NEAR stolen in the Binance hack remained unmoved. Elliptic valued those funds at $43.7 million.

#5108. North Korea Is Minting Its Own Meme Coins to Launder Binance Hack Loot

Elliptic says North Korean launderers created six throwaway meme tokens on NEAR between Nov. 5 and 7, moving about $430,000 of stolen Binance funds through homemade liquidity pools.

North Korean launderers are creating their own worthless meme coins and pushing stolen Binance funds through them so the coins never move directly from one wallet to another, blockchain analytics firm Elliptic said Monday.

Between Nov. 5 and 7, the operators minted at least six new tokens on the NEAR blockchain — named FUK, OLCCL, MEILI, MMLI, DABI and SINU — paired each with NEAR in its own liquidity pool, then bought the tokens with stolen NEAR and sold them back out through a second set of wallets. About 117,000 NEAR, or roughly $430,000, passed through the six tokens, Elliptic wrote.

The trail sits inside the broader laundering of the Oct. 29 Binance theft, which the exchange has put at $6.34 billion and which the FBI has attributed to North Korea’s TraderTraitor group. Elliptic’s wider update now puts roughly 3,000 BTC — about $225 million — in new bitcoin addresses converted from other stolen assets, while about 11.8 million NEAR, or $43.7 million, from the theft has still not moved.

“Most people think of meme tokens as joke coins whose prices rise and fall on hype,” Elliptic co-founder and chief scientist Dr. Tom Robinson wrote. “North Korea’s launderers have found a different use for them.”

The technique is a four-step hop. The North Korea launderers create a token with a public launch tool, open a liquidity pool pairing it with a real asset such as NEAR or a stablecoin, buy the new token with stolen funds from one set of wallets, then sell it back into the same pool from a second set of wallets that collect the real asset.

At no point do the stolen funds transfer directly from a buying wallet to a collecting wallet, because the pool sits in between. To a casual observer, the collecting wallets look like ordinary traders who sold a token at a profit.

Each of the six NEAR tokens cost about 25 NEAR, or around $90, to create. Each was traded heavily within hours using stolen NEAR and then abandoned. None has traded since Nov. 8, and that afternoon the creators of four of them withdrew what liquidity was left. Five of the six token creators were funded by a wallet that had just sold an earlier token, between two and 13 minutes before the next launch, so the tokens form a chain.

Elliptic first reported the same approach in the laundering of the February 2025 Bybit theft, also attributed to North Korean group TraderTraitor, when a worthless Tron token helped move at least $24 million. In the Binance case the firm has also seen the technique on Tron and a close variant on Solana that used existing thinly traded tokens rather than homemade ones.

The meme coins do not hide the funds. Every step is public on-chain. What they buy is time. Each extra hop slows investigators while launderers race to move funds before they are flagged, and at about $90 apiece the tokens are cheap enough to throw away.

That fits the larger pattern Elliptic has tracked since the theft: more than 20,000 wallets on 16 blockchains, at least 36 services and at least 65,000 transactions. Binance has said the attack began with a fake job interview and ran for five months before any coins moved. Blockchain firms linked the breach to TraderTraitor within a day.

The conversion into bitcoin is the bigger dollar story. All of the stolen SOL has already been swapped, bridged or mixed, and much of the XRP route has fed into bitcoin as well. About $2.85 billion of the original haul has been frozen. A negligible amount has likely been cashed out so far, Elliptic said.

North Korea’s crypto theft machine is not new. Elliptic put the North Korean regime’s 2025 haul above $2 billion, driven by the $1.46 billion Bybit exploit, and its cumulative known total above $6 billion even before Binance.

Binance has reopened withdrawals in stages and said it covered customer losses from its own funds. The test for investigators is whether homemade liquidity pools keep multiplying faster than the blocklists catching up to them.

#5105. Tom Lee’s Bitmine Adds $20M in Ethereum, Seizing on Vitalik’s 'ETH Is Money' Declaration

Tom Lee’s Bitmine Adds $20M in Ethereum, Seizing on Vitalik’s 'ETH Is Money' Declaration

Bitmine Immersion Technologies extended its weekly Ethereum buying streak, adding 9,587 ETH worth roughly $20 million, as chairman Tom Lee pointed to a recent declaration by Ethereum co-founder Vitalik Buterin as validation of the firm’s accumulation strategy.

The NYSE-listed company said Monday its holdings reached 6,070,092 ETH, worth roughly $13.6 billion at a reference price of $2,234 per coin.

That’s 4.97% of Ethereum’s 122.2 million token supply, leaving Bitmine, by its own count, 99% of the way to its goal of owning 5% of the network, a target it calls the “Alchemy of 5%,” reached in just 15 months.

The firm has bought Ethereum every week since launching its treasury strategy on June 30, 2025.

Lee leaned heavily on comments Buterin made at the Devcon conference in Mumbai, where the Ethereum founder said, “I’ve finally made up my mind, ETH is a store of value, ETH is money.” Lee called the declaration “a seismic shift,” arguing it clarifies how ETH will capture value in what he described as a coming “supercycle of tokenization and AI.” (Disclosure: Tom Lee is one of several investors in Dastan, Decrypt’s parent company.)

He contended ETH functions as “pristine and trusted collateral” rather than a cash-flow token, reflecting its position as the layer-1 network favored by financial institutions moving assets onchain.

Buterin elaborated in an X post, laying out why he now sees ETH primarily as a store of value, including his view that fee-burning is weaker than it once seemed and that optimizing for it can conflict with decentralization. Lee framed that reasoning as consistent with Ethereum’s roadmap and its role in the tokenization wave.

Counting cash, other tokens and “moonshot” investments, Bitmine’s total holdings stood at $14.3 billion as of Nov. 8, including 216 Bitcoin, $475 million in cash and marketable securities, a $180 million stake in Beast Industries and a $75 million position in Eightco Holdings, which it bills as a rare public proxy for indirect OpenAI exposure.

Bitmine remains the world’s largest Ethereum treasury and the second-largest crypto treasury overall, trailing only Michael Saylor’s Strategy, which the release said holds about 849,327 BTC. Bitmine has 5,227,037 ETH, roughly 86% of its holdings, staked through its MAVAN platform, with projected annualized staking revenue of about $359 million.

The firm also recently argued the muted market reaction to the record Binance hack showed crypto’s growing resilience.

#5103. Should you leave Australia to avoid paying 47% tax on crypto gains?

Some Australian crypto investors are moving overseas to avoid the huge CGT increase from July 2027. But there are also less drastic ways you can pay less tax.

In part one of our special on how to avoid Australia’s massive hike in capital gains taxes on crypto in July next year, we looked at borrowing against your Bitcoin or setting up a self-managed super fund (SMSF), among four ways to mitigate the impact of the taxes.While an SMSF offers Australians the greatest tax advantages, it’s only suitable for older investors, or those who are willing to lock up their crypto for decades until they retire. For younger investors, moving overseas could reduce your future crypto tax bills to 0% — but there are less drastic options to save on tax too.

Australia’s impending tax changes have already inspired crypto king Fred Schebesta, founder of Finder.com, to become a tax exile in Singapore.

From July, the top rate on real, long-term crypto gains will increase to 47%, minus the effects of inflation on the cost basis.

For upwardly mobile, single crypto investors, the “nuclear option” of leaving the country to avoid giving almost half their gains to the government will look increasingly attractive.Many of the world’s top crypto hubs — Singapore, Portugal, Malta, UAE and Hong Kong — offer 0% capital gains taxes on long-term crypto gains in an attempt to attract wealthy investors.

Also read: The one thing these 6 global crypto hubs all have in common...Should you leave Australia to avoid paying 47% tax on crypto gains?

Ben Simpson, founder of crypto advisory service Collective Shift, says some of his members have already pulled the trigger.

“Yeah, we’ve had a number of people that have sort of packed up and gone basically, ‘f—- this. I’m off to the Bahamas or whatnot,’” he says.

Andrea Yuen, CEO of Sywftx, says she is keenly aware of the tax advantages of living outside of Australia. “I lived in Singapore for 30 years. I do miss my no capital gains tax,” she says. But she didn’t move there for tax reasons, and cautions that uprooting your life to avoid a bit of tax is a pretty drastic step.

“I would never say that we should structure our lives only to think about tax outcomes,” she says. “There are many other wonderful reasons why I’ve come back to Australia and continued to live in Australia.”

Simpson has considered the idea, given he already spends half the year in America looking after Collective Shift US, but looking into the rules put him off. “You really have to cut pretty much all ties to Australia. So if you are genuinely happy to leave and don’t want to come back for the majority of the year, and cut your healthcare and cut all your ties back to Australia, then that is a strategy.”

“Bitcoin Jesus” Roger Ver gave up his US residency in 2014, and ten years later he was arrested and charged over failing to correctly report the value of his crypto holdings and paying exit taxes. He was forced to pay almost $50 million to make the case go away. Similarly, Australians leaving the country face a hefty exit tax on departure, which may require selling a decent amount of your stack.

“They tax you on the way out. If you’ve got a lot of gains or profits you’re sitting on, they’ll catch you when you leave,” says Adrian Forza, director of Crypto Tax Australia. “Arguably it’d be bigger in the future, but it’s still difficult to stomach selling off everything to leave.”

Forza explains you also have the option of paying the Australian taxes later on, but on the full value of the gain after you sell the asset. So if you expect Bitcoin to go up, you’d be best off paying the exit tax now.

Related: Australia releases draft bill to adopt OECD crypto tax reporting framework

Simpson is only 27 and the prospect of locking up his crypto portfolio for forty years holds little appeal. Instead, he trades and holds crypto via a company to benefit from the 25% company tax rate.

Running a single-person company is easier than you might think, but not worth the hassle unless you have a reasonable amount of capital and intend to make a lot of trades. It requires registering the company with ASIC and paying an annual fee, paying an accountant to produce financial statements and/or an annual company tax return and mucking about with payroll software and payday superannuation contributions if you plan to pay yourself an income. You can pay yourself a dividend at any time when you need some money.

“The company structure is good if you’re wanting to cap the capital gains tax at the company tax rate,” he says, but notes there’s a big catch: when you take funds out of the company, you need to pay the difference between the 25% company tax rate and your personal tax rate. “You still need to pay your marginal income tax rates. If you’re on the top tax rate, like 47%,” he says.

You may be wondering: if you have to pay your income tax rate anyway, which will equal the CGT rate from July, what is the benefit in paying a few thousand dollars and filling out a bunch of paperwork to run a company?

Simpson explains that the key advantage is that while you are building up your crypto portfolio by trading in and out of different coins, you only pay the 25% company rate for each sale. That means paying up to 22% less than CGT each time you exit a position, which builds into an even more significant saving over time.

“Instead of paying 47% because you’ve just taken the profits… then the CGT amount is only 25% — the company tax rate. So when you put that money back in, you’ve got more capital to play with.”

However, if you don’t trade enough, it might be taxed at 30%.

Forza says the advantages are greater for those on higher incomes.

It’s not a particularly useful structure if you just plan to just hold your Bitcoin, however. There are some potential advantages but arguably not enough of them to be worth the hassle.

The 50% CGT discount has been replaced by an indexation discount, which takes into account how much inflation affects how much you paid originally for an asset.

This means that investors who buy and hold dividend stocks or index funds for a long period will receive a bigger CGT discount. So should you drop crypto and buy a bunch of ETFs so you can pay less tax?

The short answer is no: the new CGT ‘discount’ is largely an illusion, as it simply measures your real gain after inflation.

Simpson says crypto has been hit hard by the CGT changes, but it still offers the best risk-reward opportunity.

History suggests that despite volatility and years-long bear markets, the return from investing in Bitcoin over time would still leave investors ahead of ETF investors even after paying more tax. Investing further down the risk curve with altcoins however, can either pay off handsomely or end in total disaster.

JPMorgan assumes that Bitcoin will compound by 10% on average in the years ahead. (Source JPMorgan/Bitcoin Archive)

Simpson says he convinced his younger sister, who is a basketball player on a low income, to invest in Bitcoin two years ago rather than putting money into a savings account. “She’s up like 40% or 50% in her portfolio and she’s like, ‘wow, this is going to get me so much further towards buying a house one day than sitting in cash and losing 10% [to inflation over time],” he says.

“That’s why I think Bitcoin makes so much sense to accumulate. And the tax considerations are not really there for most people I think.”

Yuen says crypto is one of the few asset classes that offers investors the chance to make double-digit returns in a few months, or to potentially double their money. Even after paying the new taxes, crypto investors with those sorts of returns will still be ahead.

“My dad always said to me, it’s a good thing, if you’ve made profits, you should be happy to pay some tax on it.”

Well maybe not happy about it, but you’ll still be making money.

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

The acquisition is expected to add a new financing layer to Securitize, allowing the platform to offer asset-backed lending on top of its existing tokenized asset offerings.

Asset tokenization firm Securitize's NYSE-listed stock Securitize Corp. (SECZ) rose significantly after markets closed on Monday following the announcement of its third-quarter results and its acquisition arrangement with River One.

Securitize reported 13% year-over-year growth in Q3 revenue to $18.6 million, compared with a 5% year-over-year decline to $14.4 million in the second quarter.

The company posted a net loss of $66.3 million, which translates into a net loss per diluted share of $0.41. Around 90% of its net loss primarily came from non-cash expenses and accounting changes associated with its business combination and public listing, Securitize said.

Average tokenized assets rose 39% from a year ago to $5 billion, hitting an internal record. The firm's board of directors also authorized a $50 million share repurchase program.

Securitize's shares, which closed Monday at $6.97, surged 12.20% in after-hours trading to $7.82.

Meanwhile, Securitize announced on the same day that it has entered into a definitive agreement to acquire the credit infrastructure company, which is developing technology to connect stablecoin capital with traditional lending markets.

The acquisition is expected to add a new layer of financing to Securitize, enabling the platform to offer asset-backed lending alongside its existing tokenized asset offerings. The transaction is expected to close in the fourth quarter.

"By combining Securitize's tokenization infrastructure with River One's warehouse financing technology, the companies aim to enable institutions to bring financial assets onchain and access financing against those assets through an integrated platform," Securitize wrote in a press release, further explaining that the transaction allows the firm to enter the $6 trillion global private asset-backed finance market.

Founded in 2025, River One was incubated by Framework Ventures and is backed by both Framework Ventures and Maple Finance. It specializes in warehouse financing backed by portfolios of income-generating assets, including loans, receivables, and other collateral.

River One is establishing ties with capital providers in the stablecoin ecosystem to finance high-quality, income-generating assets. In practice, it uses stablecoin capital to fund the facilities on the back end while borrowers draw and repay in ordinary dollars, so the crypto layer stays mostly invisible to them. This is to maintain traditional credit underwriting standards and legal protections while using blockchain to improve financing efficiency.

"River One brings a talented team, deep expertise in asset-backed financing and blockchain technology that will allow us to participate in asset origination and lending markets," said Carlos Domingo, Chairman and CEO of Securitize. "This opens up a significant new addressable market for Securitize, with opportunities for higher-value services, additional revenue streams and greater monetization across our platform."

#5100. Elizabeth Warren Plans Prediction Market Crackdown as She Prepares to Chair Senate Banking

Sen. Elizabeth Warren plans to crack down on prediction markets once she takes the Senate Banking Committee gavel in January, with a banking aide telling Punchbowl News that Warren would seek to stop the sector’s “Wild West-style lack of oversight.”

The Massachusetts Democrat is poised to chair the panel after Democrats swept both chambers of Congress last week, putting her in charge of a portfolio that covers banking, housing, the Federal Reserve and cryptocurrency. Punchbowl published the aide’s rundown of her agenda on Monday.

Warren intends to use the committee’s investigative powers for oversight of prediction markets and election betting platforms, aimed at insider trading and platform manipulation — the same fight she opened before the midterms.

That earlier push was bipartisan. On Oct. 28, Warren and Sen. John Curtis, R-Utah, wrote to Kalshi CEO Tarek Mansour and Polymarket CEO Shayne Coplan asking how the companies stop candidates, campaign staff and others with material nonpublic information from trading election contracts.

“We are particularly troubled by election-related event contracts,” the senators wrote. “Event contracts tied to elections receive prominent coverage and are treated by some observers as indicators of political momentum. There are also documented cases of candidates betting on their own races, which undermines the overall integrity of the American electoral process.”

The letter cited candidates who traded on their own contests on Kalshi, including a February 2026 California gubernatorial race, and pointed to research alleging wash trading and volume manipulation on Polymarket. It also referenced an NPR investigation of campaign insiders who said they traded ahead of polling releases.

“The identification of isolated instances of improper trading does not demonstrate that these markets are adequately protected,” Warren and Curtis wrote. “Instead, it raises serious questions about how much similar activity may be going undetected.” They asked for written answers by Election Day, Nov. 3.

Kalshi’s head of enforcement, Robert DeNault, told Business Insider that the company “proactively blocks candidates and campaign staff from trading to protect market integrity.” Polymarket did not respond to that request for comment.

Prediction markets are not a side show for bitcoin readers. Polymarket is a crypto-native venue that settled U.S. access through a CFTC-registered exchange after years offshore, while Kalshi operates as a designated contract market under the Commodity Futures Trading Commission. Both sit in the orbit of the same committee Warren is about to run.

Under Chairman Michael S. Selig, the CFTC has spent 2026 defending those platforms against state gambling laws, suing Wisconsin, New York and others to assert exclusive federal jurisdiction over event contracts on registered exchanges. A Democratic Banking chair targeting what her aide called a Wild West lands as the counterweight to that stance.

Warren has been one of crypto’s sharpest critics on the Hill. She opposed the CLARITY Act market structure bill, which failed a Senate cloture vote 49-50 on Sept. 15, and analysts have argued a Democratic Congress leaves little path for a Republican rewrite. House Democrats are already sketching their own package with a bigger SEC role.

The aide’s broader agenda for Warren also includes affordability legislation, a push to cap credit card interest rates, AI chip export controls, and oversight of Trump administration officials, Wall Street CEOs, Big Tech executives and anyone tied to the Trump family’s crypto ventures.

The test for prediction markets is no longer whether Trump-era regulators will keep clearing the way. It is whether Warren, with subpoena power and the Banking gavel, turns the Kalshi and Polymarket letter into hearings that rewrite how election and event contracts are policed.

#5098. Vitalik Buterin Moves On From ‘Ultrasound Money,’ Says ETH’s Value Is Stability

Vitalik Buterin Moves On From ‘Ultrasound Money,’ Says ETH’s Value Is Stability

Ethereum co-founder Vitalik Buterin wants people to stop valuing ETH as a fee-burning token and start treating it as a store of value, arguing that the burn was "never" as strong as it seemed. Binji Pande, founding member of Ethlabs, told Decrypt that he agrees, claiming that it is a more "future-proof store of value" than Bitcoin.

It's a notable shift in messaging from the Ethereum community, after ETH was branded as "ultrasound money" when the network started burning a portion of every transaction fee in 2021. The argument was that as more people used Ethereum, fewer coins would exist in circulation, and fewer coins meant a higher price. Now, however, Buterin claims that isn't as important as people may think.

"We should not feel sad if fee burn is < 10% of breakeven indefinitely; the value of ETH comes from elsewhere," Buterin tweeted on Sunday. That value, he argued, is stability, which comes from Ethereum being decentralized, resistant to censorship, and having strong security.

Expanding on why I believe "ETH as store of value (SoV)" makes much more sense to focus on than "ETH as fee-burning token".

1. ETH as fee-burning token never was as strong as it seemed2. Optimizing for ETH as fee-burning token would demand Ethereum and…

— vitalik.eth (@VitalikButerin) November 8, 2026

Those in favour of the fee-burning structure argued that fees gave ETH a price floor, much like a company's stock may be backed by revenue and profits. From 2021 to 2023, Ethereum burned millions of ETH every year, but Buterin said this was "never a true floor." Most of these burns, he said, came from internal crypto uses like lending and silly NFT purchases.

Connor Howe, co-founder and CEO of infrastructure provider Enso, told Decrypt that Ethereum's layer-2 expansion made the fee-burning argument worse. Connor said that a significant portion of activity has moved onto layer-2s, and users pay "almost nothing" to settle transactions. To optimize a fee-burning token, he added, you'd also want to optimise for higher gas prices—which no one wants.

"If we decide we actually want to optimize ETH for burning fees to justify a present-day ~$270B market cap, then Ethereum would essentially be functioning as a world-scale major corporation," Buterin said. In doing that, Ethereum would lose one of its major selling points: decentralization.

"I agree with Vitalik, and I also believe that ETH is best positioned as a future-proof store of value," Ethlabs' Pande told Decrypt. He highlighted that the network is censorship-resistant, that it has "great distribution," is widely available, and that it is "at the center of future growing sectors such as tokenization, Stablecoins, and DeFi."

A store of value is an asset that holds its worth over time, meaning investors can put their money into it without fearing it will disappear. Historically, gold has been seen as the ultimate store of value, and Bitcoin supporters have pitched it as a "digital gold."

Pande, however, argues that both fall short compared to Ethereum.

"Gold has held the SoV role for a long time, but given the world we live in now is much more globalized, we need increased portability, something gold simply cannot offer," Pande told Decrypt. "Storing gold is simply not useful either past a certain point; you reach physical limits."

When it comes to Bitcoin as a store of value, Pande expressed concern that it isn't "future-proof" as AI and quantum threats loom.

Ethereum has been preparing for those threats for a while. In January, the Ethereum Foundation formed a dedicated post-quantum team and, more recently, researchers have debated the potential AI-powered threats. By contrast, the Bitcoin community appears more fractured on how to tackle the quantum threat.

"Security is ingrained more deeply into Ethereum’s culture than anywhere else," Julian Ma, co-founder of Ethlabs, told Decrypt. "AI being used for cyberattacks and potentially breaking cryptography strengthens ETH's position as a programmable store-of-value asset because the years of preparation and large investments into security and formal verification of the Ethereum protocol and its applications are about to pay off."

Pande added that newer assets can’t compete with ETH as a store of value, because there’s no way to verify they’re fairly distributed. The Ethereum Foundation owns less than 0.2% of ETH, he said, and the token has traded for 11 years.

“Trying to distribute or mine a new asset as a SoV in the lord’s 2026 is like trying to hide a sack of meat in a sea of piranhas,” he said. “It can and WILL get centralized.”

Not everyone thinks Ethereum’s leadership got here quickly enough, though. Howe said the Ethereum Foundation “needs to start to actually listen and look at the applications building on top of their network.”

“I think he is right,” Howe said of Buterin's recent tweet, “and I think it took too long to say it.”

Trump Media and Technology Group reported a net income of $409.7 million in Q3, compared with a net loss of $54.8 million a year earlier.

Trump Media and Technology Group, the company behind Truth Social, swung to a profit in the third quarter of this year, driven largely by non-cash realized and unrealized gains on crypto assets.

The company reported a net income of $409.7 million in Q3 compared with a net loss of $54.8 million a year earlier, according to its latest quarterly report released Monday.

#5095. Amundi Boosts Strategy (MSTR) Stake 44% to $291 Million as Shares Slide

Amundi, Europe’s largest asset manager, raised its stake in Strategy (MSTR) by about 44% in the third quarter, a Form 13F filed Nov. 6 shows, lifting the position to 1,903,725 shares worth $291.4 million at Sept. 30 prices.

That is up 579,672 shares from the 1,324,053 Amundi reported at June 30, when the same holding was valued at about $115.1 million. The Paris-based firm managed €2.579 trillion in assets at the end of September, up 11% year over year, and described itself as Europe’s leading asset manager.

The filing lands as MSTR has given back much of its third-quarter run. Strategy shares closed at $153.09 on Sept. 30 and near $126 on Monday, an 18% drop that would put Amundi’s disclosed stake near $239 million at the latest close, Yahoo Finance data show.

A 13F is a snapshot, not a trading diary. Amundi filed a combination report that folds in 17 other managers across its group, so the Strategy line can include client, fund and discretionary positions rather than a single proprietary bet, and the firm may have changed the holding since Sept. 30.

Strategy itself kept stacking. The company bought another 1,036 bitcoin last week for $81.9 million at an average of $79,012, a Monday 8-K filing and company release show, lifting its treasury to 850,364 BTC acquired for $64.16 billion at an average of $75,456.

That average cost now sits above the spot bitcoin price, leaving the stack underwater on paper even after the firm added 811 bitcoin the prior week. Strategy’s public ledger still ranks it as the largest corporate bitcoin holder, with the stack equal to roughly 4% of bitcoin’s 21 million supply cap.

MSTR roughly doubled during the third quarter itself. The June 30 13F valued Amundi’s then-smaller stake at an implied $86.93 a share, against the $153.09 Sept. 30 close, so the dollar value of the position rose faster than the share count. Since then the stock has tracked bitcoin’s slide into the mid-$70,000s, wiping out a large share of that mark-to-market gain for anyone still holding the Sept. 30 position.

The timing matters because Strategy funds most of its bitcoin buys by selling new MSTR common under an at-the-market program. A weaker share price raises more dilution for every coin purchased, which is the feedback loop that has defined the stock’s moves this year.

Amundi was not the only large European name adding bitcoin exposure on the quarter. Barclays’ own 13F, also filed Nov. 6, shows 3,942,900 shares of BlackRock’s iShares Bitcoin Trust (IBIT) worth $186.7 million at Sept. 30, up 48% from 2,658,147 shares three months earlier, SEC records show.

That Barclays line carries the same caveat: a 13F gathers client, managed and trading book positions, not proof the bank put its own capital into bitcoin. Still, the two filings together sketch the same picture of Q3: traditional European managers kept adding exposure to bitcoin proxies even as the spot market cooled into the fall.

The institutional adds sit against a weaker tape since quarter-end. Bitcoin has slipped into the mid-$70,000s, Strategy stock has followed it down, and the value of Amundi’s disclosed MSTR stake has compressed even as the share count on the filing stayed fixed. At Monday’s close the disclosed stake was about $52 million below its Sept. 30 mark.

Whether Amundi kept buying, trimmed, or simply held through October’s slide will not show up until the next 13F. For now the Nov. 6 filing is the record Europe’s biggest asset manager left: nearly 1.9 million Strategy shares, up 44%, at a $291 million quarter-end mark.

#5094. HIVE, MARA mine more Bitcoin but earn less as crypto prices fall

Both miners reported higher Bitcoin production but lower mining revenue and rising electricity costs as they expanded their AI infrastructure businesses.

Update (Nov. 10, 2026, 7:31 am UTC): This article has been updated to include details of MARA’s $1.1 billion Bitcoin sale and its repayment of $750 million in Bitcoin-backed loans.

HIVE Digital Technologies and MARA Holdings mined more Bitcoin in the September quarter than a year earlier but reported lower mining revenue, as weaker Bitcoin prices outweighed gains in production.

According to their latest regulatory filings, HIVE’s Bitcoin production increased 49% to 1,070 Bitcoin (BTC), while mining revenue fell 7.4% to $76 million. MARA reported a similar pattern, with production rising 5% to 2,247 BTC and Bitcoin mining revenue dropping 34% to $159 million.

MARA also disclosed that it sold 13,597 BTC for about $1.1 billion after the quarter ended Sept. 30. It used $750 million of the proceeds to repay its Bitcoin-backed term loans in full, releasing 17,477 BTC it had pledged as collateral.

Both companies reported higher power bills and equipment charges. HIVE’s electricity costs rose 20% to $45.6 million, while MARA’s purchased energy costs increased 12% to $48.1 million as it expanded its owned mining operations.

HIVE also recorded a $31.2 million noncash write-down on mining equipment, citing “depressed mining economics,” while MARA separately wrote off $12 million involving mining rigs and equipment under restructuring plans.

Both results add to signs of pressure on Bitcoin mining economics. American Bitcoin last week reported weaker mining margins despite a treasury-driven quarterly profit, while Core Scientific said it plans to leave mining entirely by year-end.

Unlike Core Scientific, HIVE and MARA are both continuing to invest in Bitcoin mining alongside artificial intelligence and high-performance computing.

HIVE’s HPC hosting revenue rose 71% to $8.8 million in the September quarter, accounting for 10.4% of total revenue. The company has described its strategy as combining cash flow from mining with recurring revenue from AI infrastructure.

Related: American Bitcoin’s $126M profit masks mining pressure as rivals turn to AI

HIVE said it anticipates spending up to $493 million over 36 months to expand its HPC operations, including acquiring land, building out data center infrastructure and buying up GPUs. The company reported spending $145.3 million toward that expansion as of the filing.

HIVE also plans to spend about $35 million converting its Toronto facility into a Tier-III data center. Meanwhile, it continues to expand its mining infrastructure, targeting a 100-megawatt substation expansion at its Yguazú site in Paraguay during 2026.

MARA is also developing AI infrastructure through its Exaion subsidiary and a partnership with Starwood Digital Ventures. It said it intends to operate sites that can shift between workloads depending on economics, demand and grid conditions, with Bitcoin mining remaining the foundation of its platform.

HIVE’s Nasdaq-listed shares fell 1.7% to $1.78 in after-hours trading Monday, according to Google Finance, after closing the regular session up 1.7% at $1.81.

The company reported fiscal second-quarter revenue of $84.9 million, down 2.7% from a year earlier but above the $79.4 million consensus estimate listed by MarketBeat.

Meanwhile, MARA reported net income attributable to common stockholders of $634.4 million, up from $123.1 million a year earlier, and included substantial fair-value gains on digital assets and digital asset receivables.

MARA reported quarterly revenue of $164.2 million, down 35% year-on-year, and diluted earnings of $1.28 a share, up from 27 cents, while its shares were little changed at $6.64 in after-hours trading Monday after closing up 4.6%.

#5092. Solana Set for Another Tokenized Stock Venue as Formation Notifies the SEC

Solana Set for Another Tokenized Stock Venue as Formation Notifies the SEC

A new firm is lining up to become one of the first to trade tokenized U.S. stocks onchain, notifying the Securities and Exchange Commission that it intends to run a tokenized securities venue on Solana.

Formation Securities said Monday it had filed the notice to operate under the SEC’s “Innovation Exemption,” the conditional relief the agency issued in September that lets eligible tokenized stocks trade through permissioned automated market maker pools on qualifying venues.

Trading is expected to begin in early 2027, with tokenized shares of Exodus Movement, Forward Industries and Galaxy Digital anticipated at launch.

The pitch is to deliver the benefits of blockchain trading, 24/7 markets and near-instant settlement, without stripping away the rights investors expect from real stock.

Under the exemption, eligible tokens must carry the same voting and dividend rights as traditional shares, and each trade settles atomically onchain, a feature meant to cut settlement risk. A company’s transfer agent still keeps the official shareholder record, while every trade posts to the public ledger in real time.

“Tokenized stocks will succeed only if public companies see the value in them, and we intend to build this market together with issuers,” said Christopher Montagano, president of Formation Securities, which licenses its trading technology from Solana-based exchange Orca.

Tokenization firms Securitize and Superstate are expected to supply tokenized shares, with Securitize CEO Carlos Domingo noting its tokens are each backed by a real share held by its broker-dealer.

Access will be limited to verified investors, who can reach the venue through Formation’s platform and distributors including Alpaca, CoinList’s Passage, custodians Anchorage, BitGo and Fireblocks, and wallet providers Solflare and Squads.

The move adds another entrant to a fast-crowding race to put stocks onchain. The NYSE recently tapped Solana rival Avalanche to settle its own planned tokenized securities platform, Securitize launched tokenized stocks on Solana, and an OKX-ICE venue is also in the works, though every Magnificent Seven stock opted out of that one.

The effort builds on Project Open, a proposal a coalition including Orca, Superstate and the Solana Policy Institute submitted to the SEC in 2025.

Formation chose Solana, it said, for its speed, low costs and track record with tokenized assets, noting the network hosts more than $5 billion in tokenized real-world assets.

#5091. Coinbase, Kalshi and Payward backs CFTC in lawsuit over single-stock perp futures

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

The crypto firms have asked the court for permission to file an amicus brief defending the CFTC's authority to regulate single-stock perpetual futures in the US.

A group of major crypto firms is seeking federal court permission to file an amicus brief defending the CFTC's authority to regulate single-stock perpetual futures in the U.S.

Coinbase, Kalshi, Kraken parent Payward, DRW Holdings, Jump Trading, and Paradigm have moved for leave to file the brief to support the Commodity Futures Trading Commission, according to a Monday court filing.

The brief comes in response to a lawsuit filed last week by the Capital Markets Alliance (CMA) seeking to overturn the CFTC's approval of the products. The CMA is an industry association with members including Cboe and Citadel Securities.

According to the Monday filing, Coinbase, Kalshi, and Payward have submitted applications with the CFTC to list equity perps tied to U.S. stocks, and the applications were deemed approved on Nov. 2.

Perpetual futures, also known as perps, are derivatives that allow traders to speculate on an asset's price movements without holding the underlying asset. Unlike traditional futures contracts, they have no expiration date.

In CMA's view, equity perps do not expire on a set date, so they should not be treated as futures but rather as "security-based swaps" that can't be listed on CFTC-regulated exchanges. The association claimed that the CFTC overstepped the Securities and Exchange Commission's authority.

The crypto firms, however, pushed back against that argument, saying the SEC was "at the table from the very start" of the regulatory process.

"The agencies jointly sought public comment on whether perpetual contracts should be classified as security futures," the Monday filing said.

Molly Abraham, general counsel of Coinbase, wrote on X that under the Commodity Exchange Act and the securities futures framework Congress established in 2000, security futures products are not swaps. Separately, Coinbase Vice Chairman Ryan VanGrack added that global markets are already trading equity perps.

"U.S. investors deserve access to these markets under the strong consumer protections and joint oversight of the CFTC and SEC," VanGrack said. "The U.S. should lead, not cede, this space."

#5089. Bitcoin Price Trades Down Near $73,500 After Hot CPI Print Lifts Fed Hike Bets

Bitcoin price slipped to $73,500 Tuesday morning after October consumer prices rose 0.4%, topping forecasts and pushing traders toward a bigger Federal Reserve rate hike in December.

Bitcoin price traded near $73,500 on Tuesday morning, down about 1% over the past day and roughly $32,000 below its price one year ago, after a hotter-than-expected inflation report revived fears of a larger Federal Reserve rate hike, according to Bitcoin Magazine Pro data.

The U.S. consumer price index rose 0.4% in October, above the 0.3% economists expected. Core CPI, which strips out food and energy, climbed 0.3% against forecasts for 0.2%.

Bitcoin price had been holding near $74,300 in the minutes before the 8:30 a.m. ET release. It dropped to about $73,400 right after the print, briefly undercutting Monday’s overnight low of $73,477, before steadying in the high-$73,000s.

JUST IN: U.S. CPI inflation comes in at 0.4% MoM, higher than expectations 👀Bitcoin falls below $74,000 on the news. pic.twitter.com/Zxq3LtVwAd

Food prices jumped 0.5% on the month, energy rose 0.7% and gasoline gained 1.0%. Housing costs increased 0.3%.

The report will not carry an annual inflation rate. The government shutdown in October 2025 kept the Bureau of Labor Statistics from collecting a full set of prices, so there is no comparable figure. Bank of America estimated before the release that the annual rates were running near 3.8% for headline and 2.7% for core.

Bond traders moved first. Two- to five-year Treasury yields rose at least 10 basis points on the day after the print, and traders boosted bets on a half-point hike when the Fed meets on Dec. 9.

That was the scenario Fed Governor Christopher Waller laid out hours earlier. He said he is “leaning toward” a December increase and would back “more robust action” if inflation ran hotter than expected.

“In that case, I believe a 50 basis point hike in the policy rate should be considered at the December meeting,” Waller said. “Hiking only 25 basis points would put our credibility at risk, a risk I am not eager to take.”

Before the data, markets had already largely priced in a quarter-point move. Higher rates raise the cost of holding an asset that pays no yield, and that math has capped the bitcoin price since Friday’s strong jobs report.

Bitcoin had already been soft overnight. It lost about 1.4% from midnight UTC before the data, even as Asian markets rallied. South Korea’s Kospi jumped 4.39% and Japan’s Nikkei 225 rose 2.87%, while oil kept falling after President Donald Trump called fresh strikes on Iran “highly unlikely.”

Stock futures were mixed going into the release. Dow futures rose 156 points, or 0.3%, while Nasdaq-100 futures slipped 0.4% after Monday’s rally gave the S&P 500 its best day since April 8.

Commodities slipped too. West Texas Intermediate crude traded near $84 a barrel, its lowest since late August, and gold fell just over 1% to below $4,100 an ounce.

Fund demand has not cushioned the drop. U.S. spot bitcoin ETFs shed $201 million on Monday, led by $213.2 million leaving BlackRock’s iShares Bitcoin Trust (IBIT), its seventh straight session of redemptions. The streak comes even as BlackRock says demand to swap coins into IBIT in kind is at a record.

The bitcoin price has drifted lower since peaking above $87,000 in early October, as AI-related jitters weighed on Wall Street and ETF buyers stepped back.

Ether fell about 1.4% to near $2,100 overnight, as U.S. spot ether ETFs lost $184.6 million on Monday.

Analysts flagged $73,000 as the line to watch. A daily close below it could open the way to $70,000, while reclaiming $80,000 would point to stronger demand.

Producer price data lands Friday, followed by minutes from the Fed’s October meeting on Nov. 18. Both will help decide whether traders hold onto bets on a half-point hike or pare them back.

At the time of writing, the bitcoin price is near $73,500.

#5086. Nvidia's $350 Billion Scare: What Happens If Jensen Huang Leaves?

Nvidia's $350 Billion Scare: What Happens If Jensen Huang Leaves?

Nvidia shares plunged as much as 8% Monday, briefly threatening to erase roughly $350 billion in market value as investors weighed the possibility that CEO Jensen Huang could lose his job over an alleged Chinese intelligence operation.

For a company that has become the face of the artificial intelligence boom, that's a rather expensive scandal. And the investigation is just getting started.

Nvidia's board opened an independent investigation following reports that Huang had a romantic relationship with a business executive whom U.S. intelligence believes was recruited by China's Ministry of State Security.

Huang acknowledged being unfaithful to his wife in a message to employees, apologized and pledged to cooperate. Nvidia's board said it had no reason to believe company secrets or U.S. national security had been compromised, and expressed confidence in his leadership.

The allegations have not established that Huang disclosed sensitive information or knowingly cooperated with foreign intelligence. Investors, however, are already confronting the possibility of a much bigger problem.

Nvidia's market capitalization had declined from roughly $5.78 trillion on October 6 to $4.51 trillion by November 6, according to YCharts. That's approximately $1.27 trillion in lost value before the board publicly announced its investigation.

The latest controversy adds another risk to a stock already under pressure from questions about the sustainability of massive AI infrastructure spending.

Nvidia also carries enough weight to move the broader market. A November 6 snapshot placed the company at 6.93% of the SPDR S&P 500 ETF, which tracks the market-capitalization-weighted index of major U.S. companies.

At that weight, a 5.6% decline in Nvidia alone would subtract approximately 0.39 percentage points from the S&P 500's performance, assuming everything else remained unchanged—which most likely will not happen, considering Nvidia is the main hardware provider for many other companies in the country, including some in the top 10.

Yet the S&P 500 rose 1.48% Monday morning, while the Nasdaq gained 1.87%. Other semiconductor stocks also rallied, suggesting investors were treating Nvidia's troubles as largely company-specific rather than abandoning the broader AI trade.

Huang has led Nvidia since co-founding it in 1993. Replacing him would involve considerably more than finding another executive capable of presenting a new GPU.

The 63-year-old CEO has built an unusually centralized management structure and maintains important relationships with customers, suppliers and governments. Analysts have questioned whether Nvidia has an obvious successor.

Chris Weston, head of research at Pepperstone, told Reuters he didn't expect Huang to resign. But if investors genuinely believed his departure was imminent, the stock's reaction could be an "absolute bloodbath."

Bloodbath or not, the stock would be exposed to a lot of uncertainty. After all, Huang is the man responsible for building a whole imperium. It would be similar to Mark Zuckerberg leaving Meta, Elon Musk leaving Tesla or Vitalik Buterin disowning Ethereum. Beyond pure market analysis, Huang is the name behind Nvidia and market sentiment can react to that.

Dan Ives of Yorkville Ives & Co. similarly described Huang as not replaceable, although he acknowledged the investigation could ultimately result in his removal.

An extramarital affair alone does not automatically justify dismissing a CEO. Unless the scandal becomes a reputational risk for Nvidia, it’s possible that Huang escapes the scandal and markets recover as long as the company continues being extremely profitable. The more consequential question is whether Huang's conduct exposed sensitive information, created security vulnerabilities, or compromised Nvidia's business interests.

So far, the company has announced no findings establishing such failures.

A resignation could trigger another sharp selloff, particularly without a credible succession plan. But there is no reliable way to calculate how far the stock would fall, or whether investors would ultimately welcome new management.

Nvidia has been in a sustained bullish trend ever since ChatGPT entered the market in 2023. That said, its weekly chart was already flashing warning signs before the allegations emerged.

The stock traded near $179.34 Monday, significantly below its 50-week exponential moving average of approximately $199.16. That indicator tracks the longer-term price trend while assigning greater weight to recent trading.

The breakdown is particularly significant because Nvidia had spent much of its recent bull market above that average. Its last comparable breach occurred during the 2025 correction and the pre-ChatGPT era before that in 2022-2023.

The stock had already crossed below that indicator before the scandal, meaning the investigation accelerated an existing bearish trend rather than causing the entire breakdown.

Based on the weekly chart, several levels stand out:

These are potential price targets based on technical analysis, and a recovery above $190, followed by a move back above the 50-week EMA near $199, would improve the technical outlook.

Monday's weekly candle also remains unfinished, so a confirmed weekly close below the indicator would carry more weight than an intraday move.

President Donald Trump has already defended Huang, describing him as an honorable person who has done an exceptional job for the United States.

"I think he probably has an excuse for it. You have to hear it," Trump told reporters Sunday.

The president's support could help Huang politically, but the controversy comes as Nvidia navigates Washington's restrictions on advanced AI chip exports to China.

Huang has repeatedly advocated allowing Nvidia to sell processors to Chinese customers. Allegations involving a suspected intelligence operation could complicate those negotiations, even without evidence that the CEO knowingly participated in espionage.

China's Foreign Ministry has rejected the allegations as baseless.

Congressional Republicans have also requested a private briefing on Nvidia's security procedures, with lawmakers seeking Huang's agreement to a briefing date by November 11, according to Axios.

Investors have another important date approaching. Nvidia is scheduled to report its quarterly earnings on November 17, giving the market an opportunity to assess its underlying business against the uncertainty surrounding its CEO.

The board has not disclosed when its independent investigation will conclude.

#5084. OpenAI builds kill switch to stop rogue agents within 35 seconds

Control Room cuts off model output for internal research jobs when people or automated monitors flag safety or security concerns.

OpenAI has built a system that can cut off an AI research experiment’s access to model output, following several disclosures of its models acting beyond their instructions this year.

In a blog post Monday, OpenAI called the system “Control Room,” which is a centralized system that tracks model workloads across its research infrastructure and stops them quickly when safety or security concerns arise.

“As our models get more powerful and the associated safety and security risks become bigger, it becomes more and more critical that we can detect potential issues, and quickly stop models in their tracks when needed,” said the company.

The announcement comes after OpenAI disclosed several incidents this year in which research models acted outside their instructions during evaluations. In July, the company said its models had escaped a restricted testing environment and hacked Hugging Face to cheat on a security evaluation.

In October, it disclosed that a research model had used a crypto wallet without authorization to pay for smart contract deployments using testnet Ether, then omitted the spending from its final summary.

Control Room currently covers OpenAI’s internal research workloads, such as training experiments, evaluations and data processing.

It works by assigning each research job an identifier, which the inference engine checks before generating model output. If a person or automated monitoring system orders the job stopped, Control Room revokes its authorization, blocking further output even if the underlying research program keeps running, according to OpenAI.

OpenAI said cached authorizations could delay a shutdown by up to 35 seconds. The system is also designed to reject new requests if Control Room becomes unavailable.

#5081. Bitcoin in Limbo as Stocks Rebound Ahead of US Inflation Report

Bitcoin in Limbo as Stocks Rebound Ahead of US Inflation Report

After a tumultuous week across traditional and digital asset markets, Bitcoin has returned to prices last seen in early August, when the cryptocurrency mounted an impressive 25% gain in a single month.

This time, though, $75,000 looks a bit different for crypto investors, now that BTC is on the way back down. So where does Bitcoin go next? Here’s what the charts look like.

U.S. stocks rebounded Monday as oil prices fell, with the S&P 500 up 1.9% and the Nasdaq Composite up 2.2% by 11:30 a.m. Eastern. Brent crude fell 4.3% to about $94 a barrel, giving the broader market a lift even as Nvidia shares slid in a move that spooked tech investors.

The Bureau of Labor Statistics is due to release the October consumer price index tomorrow. The report tracks what households pay for goods and services, and it lands after a stronger-than-expected jobs report pushed traders to price in roughly an 80% chance of a quarter-point Federal Reserve interest rate hike in December.

Bitcoin was up 0.7% at $74,559 after touching its lowest level in more than two months. The rebound followed a wave of forced selling in crypto derivatives, with a CoinGlass snapshot showing $1.2 billion in liquidations over 24 hours.

Exchanges liquidate positions when traders can no longer cover their losses with the collateral they’ve posted. About $1.04 billion of the total came from longs, or bets on higher prices. Bitcoin positions accounted for $324 million, nearly 87% of it in longs.

Bitcoin rallied from roughly $57,700 to $87,350 before sliding back toward $75,000. The coin is currently trading at $74,685 after an early dip to $73,667, with the week’s candle still open.

The 50-week exponential moving average, or EMA, sits near $78,250, above the 200-week EMA at about $69,790. Both averages give more weight to recent prices, with the shorter one responding faster.

Their order still favors the longer-term uptrend, though Bitcoin is trading below the faster average, and the gap has narrowed considerably, which increases the possibility of a death cross in the future.

The Relative Strength Index, or RSI, compares recent gains with recent losses on a scale of zero to 100. At 47.8, it’s close to the midpoint, with a slight tilt toward sellers and some distance from 30, the level traders typically call oversold.

The Average Directional Index, or ADX, measures how strongly prices are trending in either direction. The chart’s reading of 21.9 is below the commonly used 25 threshold for a strong trend, even though its accompanying directional indicators give buyers a slight edge.

On the way down, $74,400 is the first area where buyers could step in. Bitcoin has already traded through it this week, so a weekly close below it would put $72,550 in view, followed by the $69,000 area around the 200-week EMA.

On a rebound, the first area where sellers could stall the move is near $76,040, followed by the 50-week EMA around $78,250. A move through that average would put $80,360 back in play, with the prior rebound high near $87,350 beyond it.

#5077. NEAR AI says SGLang vulnerability exposed user account details in security breach

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

NEAR AI explained that it found no indication that stored Private Chat conversations or uploaded files were accessed.

NEAR AI has contained a security breach after an attacker exploited a remote code execution vulnerability in the open-source SGLang inference engine running on its servers. NEAR AI is the AI product and infrastructure arm of the NEAR NEAR Protocol (NEAR) ecosystem.

According to NEAR AI's security incident report published on Monday, the attacker gained code execution on one inference server from the vulnerability and reached an internal system behind a previously deprecated Private Chat feature.

From there, the attacker downloaded account details — including email addresses, names, and in some cases plan and balance information — for approximately 100 users.

The attacker also accessed 18 agents on a legacy NEAR AI Agents deployment that was not running in the company’s confidential computing environment, including their chat history, settings, and logs. Two Cloud customers had their API keys exposed in logs, while those keys have since been revoked.

Its report says that on Nov. 8, the attacker could have observed user requests being processed on the compromised server, including those in confidential mode. However, NEAR AI found no indication that stored Private Chat conversations or uploaded files were accessed.

"Confidential computing protects data from the operator and the host. It does not protect against a vulnerability in the software running inside the environment, and that is what happened here," NEAR AI said. "The SGLang vulnerability let the party run code inside one inference server and access requests in processing on that server, including confidential ones."

The company said it has closed the access, replaced the affected credentials, and patched the vulnerability across all inference servers. Access to NEAR AI Cloud, which was paused during the investigation, has been restored.

NEAR AI also revoked active sessions and administrative credentials, rotated agent tokens, took Private Chat offline, and completed a full forensic review along with remaining credential rotations.

"We are now treating the inference software and the services around the enclave with the same rigor as the enclave itself when running security analysis, and the code, dependencies, and deployment configurations for our confidential inference stack remain public so that anyone can verify what is running," NEAR AI said.

The company has reached out to the affected users and the owners of the 18 agents individually.

#5076. Binance Hack Launderers Mint Elon Musk and Animal Meme Coins to Move Stolen Crypto

Binance Hack Launderers Mint Elon Musk and Animal Meme Coins to Move Stolen Crypto

North Korea’s hackers have found a novel use for the internet’s favorite joke tokens: laundering a slice of the record $6.4 billion stolen from Binance.

According to a report from blockchain analytics firm Elliptic, the launderers are creating their own worthless meme coins and using them to shuffle stolen funds through decentralized exchanges in a way that breaks the direct onchain link between the thief’s wallet and the one collecting the proceeds.

The launderers mint a brand-new token, pair it with a real asset like Near or a stablecoin in a liquidity pool, pay stolen funds into the pool through one set of wallets to buy the token, then sell it back through a separate set of wallets to withdraw clean-looking assets.

Because the pool sits in the middle, no stolen funds move directly from the buying wallet to the collecting one, and the receiving wallets look like ordinary traders cashing out a meme coin.

Elliptic, in research credited to co-founder Tom Robinson, said the launderers deployed the tactic on Tron and Near, with a close variant on Solana. The clearest example came on Near, where between Nov. 5 and 7 they created at least six home-made tokens, each paired with Near in its own pool.

The coins carried the usual meme-coin trappings: cartoon logos of a bow-wearing monkey, a flag-draped duck, a chimpanzee and a fox, one even featuring an “Elon 2028 President” image of Elon Musk, who has no connection to the tokens. The names ran from the crude (FUK) to a riff on dog coins (SINU).

Each token cost about 25 Near tokens, roughly $90, to create. All six followed the same arc: launched, traded heavily within hours using stolen NEAR, then abandoned, with the leftover liquidity pulled on Nov. 8.

In total, about 117,000 Near, or roughly $430,000, passed through the six tokens before much of it flowed into stablecoins or a cross-chain bridge.

The sums are modest so far, a reminder that the bulk of the haul is still parked onchain. Elliptic earlier found more than $3 billion of the stolen crypto had yet to be laundered, and that the launderers were spinning up a new wallet roughly every 25 seconds to move funds at machine speed.

Crucially, the meme-coin trick doesn’t actually hide the money. Elliptic stressed the trail remains fully traceable onchain; the maneuver only slows investigators down, buying time in what is essentially a race to move funds before they get flagged.

It’s one cheap, disposable layer among many that North Korea’s launderers stack together. Elliptic first observed the tactic last year while tracing the $1.5 billion Bybit theft, also attributed to the TraderTraitor group behind the Binance breach.

For compliance teams, the firm warned, funds arriving from a liquidity pool shouldn’t be assumed clean, especially where freshly minted tokens and a handful of trading wallets are involved.

#5074. Crypto exchanges need hard dollar caps on hot wallets, CertiK says

“Cold storage is a useful measure of online exposure, but it is not a standalone security score,” the cybersecurity firm told Cointelegraph.

Crypto exchanges should impose hard dollar limits on their hot-wallet holdings rather than relying solely on cold-storage ratios to measure security, according to cybersecurity firm CertiK.

The recommendation came as several crypto exchanges recently disclosed how much they held in cold wallets, seeking to reassure customers after the record Binance hack that targeted its hot wallets. Crypto exchange Gate reported that 88.26% of its total assets were in cold storage, while Coinbase said it keeps more than 99% of customer assets offline.

“Cold storage is a useful measure of online exposure, but it is not a standalone security score,” CertiK’s research team told Cointelegraph.

South Korea requires crypto service providers to keep at least 80% of customers’ crypto assets by value in cold storage. Singapore’s guidelines call for at least 90%, while Japan’s framework requires at least 95% and Hong Kong’s baseline for licensed crypto trading platforms is 98%.

CertiK said that while higher cold-storage levels generally reduce online exposure, the benefit becomes less significant at very high levels.

Related: Analysts see no broader ‘contagion’ from $6.3B Binance hack

Rigid storage ratios could also slow withdrawals, force rushed transfers and concentrate risk in key-management systems.

CertiK said crypto exchanges should consider setting hot-wallet caps for each asset and blockchain according to the strictest of three limits: a percentage of customer liabilities, estimated withdrawal needs and an absolute loss ceiling based on the exchange’s financial resources and recovery capabilities.

“Flexibility should support operating liquidity, while hard dollar ceilings and circuit breakers limit potential loss,” they said.

Binance last week said it had replenished approximately 98% of affected tokens following the hack and would absorb the losses. In a recovery update on Wednesday, the exchange said the remaining tokens were less liquid and would take longer to acquire.

Meanwhile, North Korea-linked hackers have created memecoins to launder funds from the theft, according to Elliptic. The firm traced roughly $430,000 through six newly launched tokens on NEAR, where liquidity pools helped obscure transfers between wallets, though the transactions remained traceable onchain.

#5067. Google Takes Aim at Nvidia With New AI Chips Built for Privacy and Massive Memory

The TPU 8tx and TPU 8i Max join Google’s eighth-generation Tensor Processing Unit lineup, giving businesses four configurations for training and running AI models. Google says it serves nearly 100 TPU customers, from AI labs to universities.

Unlike Nvidia’s widely used graphics processing units, TPUs are purpose-built AI accelerators. Google developed them for its own services but increasingly sells access to outside companies, including on-premises customers.

“With computing moving from human to agent scale, businesses are asking for accelerators that can reason across AI models’ massive context windows,” wrote Amin Vahdat, Google’s chief technologist and senior vice president of AI and infrastructure, in Google's announcement.

TPU 8tx targets a problem that many serious businesses have when it comes to adopting AI: protecting sensitive information while a model is using it.

Data is encrypted in storage and during transmission, but processing typically requires decrypting it in memory. Google says the configuration uses hardware-level confidential computing to isolate customer data and proprietary model parameters even during operation.

The system combines TPU 8t silicon with fifth-generation AMD EPYC processors and Google’s low-latency Boardfly network. Google claims it can cut latency by up to half versus TPU 8t, with almost no performance penalty for the added protection.

Companies could fine-tune and run models on the same system instead of transferring sensitive information between clusters. However, confidential computing cannot prevent a poorly designed AI agent from mishandling data it is allowed to access.

TPU 8i Max addresses another limitation: memory. Autonomous agents analyzing data, codebases, documents or hours of video, etc; need fast access to far more information than conventional AI tasks.

Google combines two TPU 8i chips into one package, doubling computing power, on-chip SRAM and high-bandwidth memory throughput versus TPU 8i. The design packs twice as much silicon into the same rack footprint.

“Our fourth system, TPU 8i Max, is built for a different problem: the memory wall,” Vahdat wrote.

Google is open-sourcing TPU Sync, software that moves AI working memory between chips, servers and storage. It claims the system can reduce certain data-transfer delays and response startup times by more than 90%.

Google's announcement comes as Nvidia faces mounting pressure on Wall Street. Shares plunged 9.3% on Thursday and fell another 5% in Monday's trading after CEO Jensen Huang admitted to an extramarital affair with a woman suspected by U.S. intelligence of spying for China. Nvidia's board has launched an independent investigation, although the company says it has no evidence that corporate secrets were compromised.

The scandal adds to concerns about Nvidia's growth as competitors target its AI chip dominance. OpenAI is reportedly planning to purchase Google's TPUs to reduce its reliance on Nvidia. This is still unconfirmed, but Google's expanding chip lineup could give AI developers more bargaining power, threatening Nvidia's market share and future growth expectations.

Google is competing on specialized hardware and software, not just chip speed. Its open-source TorchTPU project also lets developers run PyTorch and Hugging Face models on TPUs without rewriting their applications.

Still, Google’s performance figures compare its new systems with earlier Google hardware, not Nvidia’s latest products. The company did not disclose pricing or availability dates for the two new configurations.

#5063. Securitize agrees to buy River One to expand into asset-backed lending

Securitize agreed to buy River One to bring stablecoin funding to asset-backed lending and expects the deal to close in the fourth quarter of 2026.

BlackRock-backed tokenization platform Securitize agreed to buy River One, a New York financing firm, in a deal that would expand the tokenization company into asset-backed lending, the company said Monday.

The deal would add a new financing layer to Securitize’s platform, expanding into asset-backed lending, according to a Monday release.

Securitize announced the deal alongside Q3 results. Revenue rose 13% from a year earlier to $18.6 million, while the company posted a $66.3 million net loss. Securitize said about $60 million of the loss reflected largely noncash expenses and accounting charges tied mainly to its business combination and public listing.

#5059. AI Data Centers Are Buying Used Jet Engines as New Power Turbines Take Six Years

AI Data Centers Are Buying Used Jet Engines as New Power Turbines Take Six Years

AI data-center developers are hunting for used gas turbines and retired jet engines because ordering brand-new power equipment could leave their servers waiting for electricity until the next decade.

Some companies are buying refurbished machines, while others are converting aircraft-engine technology into generators that can run alongside their computing facilities.

New gas turbines now take an average of six years to deliver in the United States, according to energy consultancy Wood Mackenzie. Delivery discussions are already focused on 2030 and 2031, a timeline that clashes with an AI industry trying to bring enormous computing campuses online much sooner.

Global turbine orders have topped 100 gigawatts, compared with manufacturers' estimated annual capacity of just 60 to 70 gigawatts, Reuters reported. Siemens Energy, GE Vernova, and Mitsubishi Heavy Industries have all said orders are outpacing what they can produce.

Gas turbines can generate large amounts of electricity by burning natural gas to spin equipment connected to a generator. AI data centers need that power around the clock to run densely packed chips and cooling systems, and connecting new facilities to public electricity grids can itself take years.

The International Energy Agency expects global data-center electricity consumption to nearly double from 485 terawatt-hours in 2025 to 950 in 2030. Consumption at AI-focused facilities is projected to triple over the same period.

One way around the backlog is an aeroderivative turbine, a power generator built using technology adapted from aircraft jet engines.

Other companies are going further by seeking older machines and retired aircraft engines that can be rebuilt for stationary power production. For example, AI infrastructure firm Crusoe ordered 13 units from Missouri-based ProEnergy in April, each rated at 50 megawatts, for a combined 650 megawatts. The jet-engine-derived equipment is intended for future U.S. data centers, but the contract doesn’t mean that Crusoe bought engines removed from retired aircraft.

Second-hand machines can also carry a substantial discount. Used turbines in good condition sell for about $1.5 million to $1.7 million per megawatt, compared with roughly $2 million to $2.5 million for new units, according to equipment brokers and energy analysts interviewed by Reuters.

One broker described a sale of two smaller turbines totaling 29 megawatts for $12.5 million. Equivalent new equipment would have cost about $35 million and taken five years to arrive, he said.

“That secondary market is very, very hot right now,” EthosEnergy CEO Ana Amicarella told Reuters, adding that asking prices are climbing as more buyers compete for the same machinery.

Building a power plant beside a data center also brings fuel needs, air emissions, and permits into the equation. The Environmental Protection Agency says stationary turbines and engines used by data centers are subject to federal air-pollution standards.

Last week, OpenAI promised to cover electricity and grid costs at future campuses it owns and operates after voters backed tighter restrictions on data-center construction.

Power-market research firm Cleanview identified about 90 gigawatts of proposed on-site generation at U.S. data centers in its mid-2026 analysis. Only about 2 gigawatts was operating at the time

Developers are looking beyond turbines, too. Finnish manufacturer Wärtsilä told Reuters it had booked around 3 gigawatts of orders from U.S. data centers since spring 2025 for generators powered by gas engines rather than turbines.
