#5055. eToro’s Q3 profit plunges 74% as weaker crypto trading weighs on earnings

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

October crypto trades also fell 62% year over year to 2 million, while the trading platform announced a $250 million share buyback program.

Crypto and stock trading platform eToro (ETOR) reported a 74% year-over-year decline in third-quarter net income to $15 million, as weaker crypto trading weighed on its earnings.

The Nasdaq-listed company's net contribution fell 13% to $186 million from $215 million a year earlier, primarily due to reduced crypto trading activity, the firm said in its latest earnings release on Tuesday.

Adjusted EBITDA fell 56% to $34 million from $78 million in the same quarter last year. Revenue from crypto assets dropped to $1.35 billion from $3.97 billion a year earlier, and the associated cost of crypto asset revenue was $1.29 billion, compared with $3.89 billion for the same period in 2025.

In August, The Block reported that eToro had posted $53 million in second-quarter net income on $229 million in net contribution. Both figures were down from its record first quarter, although net contribution grew year over year on stronger equities trading. However, its first-quarter results had already shown a 50% year-over-year drop in crypto trades.

Beyond the third quarter, eToro's October figures showed crypto trades falling 62% year over year to 2 million. The average invested amount per crypto trade also declined 29% to $228 during the month, the firm said on Tuesday. Trading activity was also lower across equities, commodities, and currencies, with 48.2 million trades recorded during the month, down 22% from October 2025.

The platform ended September with 4.39 million funded accounts, an 18% increase from a year earlier, and assets under administration were unchanged at $20.8 billion. eToro's board also approved a $250 million share buyback program, with purchases subject to market conditions and the company's liquidity requirements.

The broader Bitcoin ETF market shed another $201 million, as $22 million in inflows to the Morgan Stanley Bitcoin Trust ETF (MSBT) partially offset the losses. The funds have recorded around $1.5 billion in net outflows over the past six trading sessions.

The Crypto Fear & Greed Index. Source: Alternative.me

Outflows from US spot Ether ETFs accelerated to roughly $185 million Monday, their largest daily withdrawals since Oct. 6.

#5051. Anthropic's Claude Fable 5.2 and Mythos 5.0 Are Impressive, But Be Prepared to Sell a Kidney to Afford Them

Anthropic's Claude Fable 5.2 and Mythos 5.0 Are Impressive, But Be Prepared to Sell a Kidney to Afford Them

Anthropic released Claude Fable 5.2 and Mythos 5.2 on Monday, promising AI models that can turn a handful of instructions into working software, design physical objects, and tackle research problems that previously required teams of specialists.

You can now ask a chatbot to build the app you've always wanted. You may also need to sell a kidney to pay for it.

That's not because every prompt costs thousands of dollars. It's because Fable can keep working for days, writing code, testing it, and fixing its own mistakes while the meter keeps running.

Some users have already tested its capabilities, and they’ve been impressed—by the power, and the price.

AI YouTuber Matthew Berman put the model through a series of ambitious tests and described Fable 5.2 as the “best AI model” he's ever used. His projects included playable video games and a browser-based version of Microsoft Excel, complete with formulas and pivot tables.

One single level of Gears of War: E-Day-inspired level took 43 hours and 48 minutes of build time and processed 8.63 billion tokens—small chunks of information an AI reads and produces. The running API cost estimate, based on pay-as-you-go pricing for developers, had reached $14,710.

I've burned over 100 billion tokens early testing Fable 5.2. It is BY FAR the best model I've ever used.

Here's my full review (including Gears of War clone):

> Fable 5.2 is the most autonomous model to date. Given a task with little direction, it makes… pic.twitter.com/0xTzqTY040

— Matthew Berman (@MatthewBerman) November 9, 2026

The Excel clone ran up an estimated $11,700 after discounts for cached inputs, or information the system can reuse instead of processing from scratch. Berman also showed a Fall Guys-style game that consumed more than 9 billion tokens over 71 hours, equivalent to roughly $13,000.

Those are estimated API costs, and the projects weren't flawless: Berman reported awkward animations, bugs, and features that still needed testing.

If you’re poor, well… Fable also knows how to work for cheap. For less than $40 (two months of Claude Pro) it built a cute orange cylinder.

Another experiment simulated water-balloon physics for $91. It’s worth noting this isn’t a model intended for routine tasks. This is for serious work and people with deep pockets.

In its launch announcement, Anthropic said the new models generate output up to 30% faster than their predecessors. The company also says Fable helped solve two open theoretical-physics problems involving black holes and design three functioning electronic devices, claims that will need independent scrutiny.

Anthropic's internal FrontierCode 2.0 evaluation, which measures the percentage of proposed coding changes that would be accepted into real open-source projects, put Fable 5.2 at 49.1%, compared with 32.4% for Fable 5.1. That helps explain why users are pushing it beyond simple chat responses.

Fable and Mythos share the same underlying model, but Fable has stricter safeguards around dangerous biological and cybersecurity requests. Mythos is reserved for verified organizations working in those fields.

The standard API price is $8 per million input tokens and $40 per million output tokens, down from the previous generation's $10 and $50. Optional fast mode can generate output up to 2.5 times faster, but doubles the rates to $16 and $80.

For contrast, Kimi 3.1 Pro, a model considered by many as the best Chinese AI model costs $2 per million input tokens and $10 per million output tokens. The latest Deepseek model costs $0.6 and $1.98 outside of peak hours.

Per Anthropic’s standards, Fable’s price is still twice the per-token price of Claude Opus 5.5, which Anthropic recommends for everyday work. Its flagship is meant for problems the cheaper models struggle to finish, not for asking whether your email sounds too aggressive.

Just last week, speculation swirled around a supposed Fable 5.5. The actual release is 5.2, with cheaper token rates but enough appetite to turn a hobby project into a serious expense.

Anthropic, last we checked, doesn’t accept kidneys as a method of payment.

#5049. Visa adds Monad to stablecoin settlement network options

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Payments company Visa has added the Monad blockchain network as an option to its stablecoin settlement program, allowing participating issuers and acquirers to settle through the network.

Monad joins a number of blockchains Visa already supports for the program, including Avalanche, Ethereum, Solana, Stellar, Circle's Arc, Base, Canton, Polygon and Tempo. Visa announced support for the latter five in April.

Blockchain settlement allows funds to move on weekends and holidays as well, compared with traditional card settlement, which runs on a five-business-day banking schedule, the Monad Foundation said.

"Our goal is to give our partners the flexibility to settle on the blockchain networks that best meet their needs," Dan Roesbery, Visa's vice president of global onchain partnerships and GTM, said in a statement. "Adding support for Monad expands the options available to our partners and is another step toward building a multi-chain settlement infrastructure for stablecoin-enabled payments."

Visa reported an annualized stablecoin settlement run rate of $20 billion as of September 2026, up more than 15x year over year. It also had more than 225 stablecoin-linked card programs live globally.

The feature is being built directly into Monad, so private transactions can use the same liquidity as other transactions on the network, the foundation said, adding that Monad can support this without slowing down settlement.

"The next phase will be defined by whether institutions can keep sensitive flows confidential without leaving open networks," Keone Hon, co-founder and general manager of the Monad Foundation, said in a statement.

Visa has also been exploring private settlement on Canton. In June, it announced a proof of concept with stablecoin issuer Brale to test settlement using its dollar-backed SBC token. The initiative focuses on how institutions can settle onchain while controlling who can see sensitive transaction data.

#5045. Bitdeer reports record Q3 revenue as net loss widens to $125.5 million

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

The miner's net loss widened to $125.5 million despite higher mining output and AI Cloud revenue lifting quarterly sales to a record $320.7 million.

Bitdeer reported record third-quarter revenue of $320.7 million, up 89% from a year earlier, as the bitcoin miner expanded its mining operations and increased AI cloud revenue.

The Singapore-based miner's net loss widened to $125.5 million from $30.1 million in the same period last year, the company said in a statement on Tuesday. The loss included a non-cash $54.6 million charge tied to changes in the fair value of a digital-asset loan.

Bitdeer CFO Michael G. Potter said the company's adjusted EBITDA, which excludes several non-cash and other items, rose 173% to $69.6 million from $25.5 million.

Bitdeer mined 3,855 BTC in the quarter, up from 1,109 BTC a year earlier, as its total proprietary hashrate climbed to 86.4 EH/s from 35 EH/s, the company said.

Self-mining revenue rose to $217.5 million from $130.9 million, while co-mining contributed another $59.9 million. The company held just 47 BTC as of Sept. 30, down from 2,029 BTC a year earlier.

Bitdeer also reported $221.5 million in proceeds from digital-asset disposals during the quarter, primarily mined bitcoin, according to the statement. It ended September with $43.2 million in digital assets and $343.8 million in digital-asset receivables from a related party, bringing the combined balance to $387 million.

Meanwhile, AI cloud revenue increased to $20.4 million from $1.8 million a year earlier. The company reported an AI cloud revenue backlog of approximately $2.9 billion and said it had secured about 332 IT megawatts of AI cloud capacity.

"We are converting AI demand into long-duration contracts," Potter said. "Our AI Cloud revenue backlog has grown to approximately $2.9 billion, and construction at Tydal remains on target. Customer prepayments, together with project-level financing, are designed to fund AI capacity as it is contracted."

Bitdeer shares closed 9.9% higher at $6.61 on Monday, according to The Block's BTDR price page. The stock was down 2.7% in premarket trading on Tuesday.

#5042. 'Tough Questions' Ahead: Warren Targets Prediction Markets, Trump Crypto as Banking Chair

'Tough Questions' Ahead: Warren Targets Prediction Markets, Trump Crypto as Banking Chair

Sen. Elizabeth Warren (D-MA), one of the crypto industry's most prominent critics, is preparing to use the chairmanship of the Senate Banking Committee to launch an oversight campaign aimed at Trump family crypto businesses, Big Tech and Wall Street, Politico reported on Monday.

"Big Tech CEOs, Administration officials, Trump family crypto businesses, Wall Street executives, and giant corporations should preserve their documents and prepare to answer tough questions," a Warren Banking Committee aide told the outlet.

Warren is expected to take over the committee in January after Democrats won control of the Senate in last week's midterm elections. The panel oversees banking, housing, cryptocurrency and the Federal Reserve, and its chair has broad powers to call hearings and launch investigations.

According to the aide, Warren also wants to end a "Wild West-style lack of oversight" of prediction markets that she says has allowed insiders to profit, and plans to warn about threats to financial stability, including a potential AI bubble.

She intends to keep working with Republican China hawks to restrict exports of advanced AI chips, and Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang is expected to be a top oversight target, Politico reported. The report came a day after Nvidia's board opened an independent investigation into reports that Huang had been the target of a Chinese intelligence effort, sending Nvidia shares sliding as much as 8%.

Warren is also likely to investigate the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency's August decision to grant a conditional bank charter to Trump-backed World Liberty Financial, as well as the administration's firings of financial agency staff and its efforts to shut down the Consumer Financial Protection Bureau, Reuters reported, citing a person familiar with her thinking. "You're going to see a lot of investigations," Brian Gardner, chief Washington policy strategist at Stifel, told the outlet.

In an essay for Time last week, Warren argued that Democrats won by "being populist economic fighters," and said "Big Tech CEOs, Wall Street executives, and giant corporations should prepare to answer tough questions and face meaningful consequences if they have broken the law."

Warren has been a persistent critic of the crypto industry and of the Trump family's business interests in it. She has argued that federal regulators' approval of national trust charters for crypto firms, including Coinbase and Ripple, is illegal, and has said the CFTC has been "steamrolled" by the prediction market and crypto firms it oversees.

She has also questioned Commerce Secretary Howard Lutnick over a Tether loan to a trust tied to his children, and joined Sen. Bernie Sanders (I-VT) in urging the Labor Department to drop plans that would make it easier to hold crypto in 401(k) retirement plans.

Warren was one of the leading opponents of the Clarity Act, the crypto industry's top legislative priority, which stalled in the Senate this year. Democrats are now planning to push a broader crypto market structure bill when they take control of Congress.

The White House has pushed back. A spokesperson told Reuters that Trump is willing to work with anyone on behalf of Americans, but that "sham investigations and radical left policies" would undermine any such effort.

Republicans have cast Warren as the main obstacle to crypto legislation. Sen. Tim Scott (R-SC), the current Banking chair, has argued that without market structure rules, crypto is left in "the wild, wild West."

Warren's ability to pass legislation will be limited while Trump remains in the White House. In her Time essay, she acknowledged that Trump has embraced the nickname "Mr. Veto," but argued that Democrats should send him bills anyway.

Warren is also eyeing a more senior role in Senate Democratic leadership, potentially the No. 3 position, Politico reported last month.

#5039. Bitcoin price eyes $74K as Fed rate-hike bets hit 96% after Waller speech

Bitcoin price action and stock futures trended lower after Fed governor Christopher Waller said that he would vote to raise interest rates by 0.25% at the December FOMC meeting.

Bitcoin (BTC) fell toward $74,000, and US stock futures dropped on Tuesday as a senior Federal Reserve official voiced support for hiking interest rates.

Data from TradingView showed BTC/USD reaching $74,080 on Bitstamp, down 1.3% on the day.

BTC/USD one-hour chart. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

US stock market futures leaned lower as Fed governor Christopher Waller said that he would support hiking interest rates by 25 basis points at the next meeting of the Federal Open Market Committee (FOMC) on Dec. 9. At the time of writing, Nasdaq 100 futures were down 0.4%.

His decision, he added, would not be swayed by lower-than-expected inflation data. The October prints of the US Consumer Price Index (CPI) is due for release at 8.30 am Eastern time on Tuesday. It is expected to show a month-on-month increase of 0.3% versus September when the increase was 0.6%, pushed higher by rising fuel costs.

“While I am hoping for an improvement over September, even if this occurs, I would still support a 25 basis point increase in the FOMC’s policy rate at our December meeting,” Waller commented in a speech at the Bank of England Watchers Conference in London.

While CPI is particularly sensitive to fuel-price volatility, Waller also flagged high readings from the Personal Consumption Expenditures (PCE) index, known to be the Fed’s “preferred” inflation gauge and which he confirmed was the “measure targeted by the FOMC.”

“Let’s just say it. September’s inflation data were bad. There was no silver lining,” he said, referring to PCE rising 0.5% month-on-month and 3.7% year-on-year, respectively.

Following the speech, markets immediately repriced odds of a 25-basis-point hike in December from 76.3% to 96.3%, per data from CME Group’s FedWatch Tool.

Fed target-rate probability comparison for December FOMC meeting (screenshot). Source: CME Group

Financial policy tightening measures traditionally act as headwinds for crypto and risk asset prices as they imply a reduction in liquidity and associated capital inflows.

Related: Bitcoin, tech stocks brush off Nvidia nerves as analyst mulls $68K target

Commenting on the impetus for the expected slowdown in CPI upside for October, Christopher Hodge, chief US economist at Natixis CIB Americas, pointed to price declines in both fuel and shelter.

“We think the slowdown in core CPI will stem from a reversion of shelter prices, primarily owners’ equivalent rents and lodging away from home. Similarly, the stabilization in diesel prices should help to quell price pressures in energy-intensive goods,” he wrote, quoted by CNBC.

The Bureau of Labor Statistics (BLS), which publishes CPI, confirmed that Tuesday’s release would include only month-on-month statistics due to the 2025 US government shutdown reducing data availability. CPI data for October 2025 was not issued for the same reason.

#5038. Nvidia, Jensen Huang Sued by Shareholders Over Alleged Ties to Suspected Chinese Spy

Nvidia, Jensen Huang Sued by Shareholders Over Alleged Ties to Suspected Chinese Spy

Nvidia and CEO Jensen Huang have been sued by a shareholder who accuses them of defrauding investors by concealing Huang's alleged relationship with a suspected Chinese spy.

The proposed class action, filed in the U.S. District Court for the Northern District of California on Monday, accuses Nvidia and Huang of making materially false and misleading statements about the company's dealings with Megaspeed, a Singapore-based Nvidia customer, according to the complaint. It alleges that Huang "had an affair with Alice Huang, a Megaspeed executive and suspected Chinese spy," and that the defendants failed to disclose that this raised national security concerns posing a material risk to Nvidia's reputation and business prospects.

The plaintiff, Mark Kistenmacher, seeks to represent investors who bought Nvidia stock between October 9, 2025, and November 8, 2026. The suit accuses Nvidia and Huang of violating Section 10(b) of the Securities Exchange Act and Rule 10b-5, and Huang of violating Section 20(a), which can hold executives liable alongside a company. It seeks unspecified damages and a jury trial. Kistenmacher is represented by The Rosen Law Firm.

The complaint targets four statements. Two came from Nvidia spokesperson John Rizzo in October 2025, when he told The New York Times that Nvidia's employees had "found no evidence of diversion" at Megaspeed and that Huang's visits to customer events were routine. A third came from Rizzo last month, when he said Huang's interactions with Alice Huang were "not unusual or unexpected." The fourth was Huang's own description of Nvidia's dealings with Megaspeed as "normal and not unusual" in an interview with Bloomberg on October 30. Each was misleading, the suit alleges, because of Huang's relationship with Alice Huang.

Alice Huang, a former Megaspeed executive, is not related to Jensen Huang, Reuters reported. The New York Times reported on Sunday that Nvidia opened its investigation after Huang acknowledged to the company's board that he had been intimate with Alice Huang. The newspaper also reported that U.S. Secretary of State Marco Rubio oversaw the drafting of a classified memo this spring warning top administration officials about the relationship, which was circulated ahead of U.S. President Donald Trump's trip to China in May.

Decrypt has reached out to Nvidia for comment. The allegations in the complaint have not been tested in court, and it is common for shareholders to sue companies after sharp share price declines.

The lawsuit was filed five minutes after U.S. markets closed on Monday, according to Reuters. Nvidia shares closed down 4.3% at $178.67 that day, according to Yahoo Finance, reducing its market value by about $190 billion, after having slid as much as 8% earlier in the session. The stock was flat on the day at $179.70 in premarket trading on Tuesday.

Nvidia's board opened an independent investigation on Sunday into reports that Huang was the target of a foreign intelligence effort, saying it had no reason to believe that national security or company secrets had been compromised. In an email to staff the same day, Huang apologized for being unfaithful to his wife, without naming the person involved.

The New York Times reported last month that U.S. officials had concluded Alice Huang was a Chinese intelligence asset, and the Financial Times later reported that a U.S. government memo said Jensen Huang had been romantically involved with her. China's embassy in Washington has dismissed the allegations as "groundless smears."

Trump weighed in on Monday. "I asked him about that and he said he had absolutely no idea that the person in question was a spy," the president told reporters, according to The Guardian. He added that the FBI was "looking into it in terms of something else," without elaborating. A day earlier, Trump told reporters he did not regret his decision not to act on the intelligence, and that he believed Huang should stay on as CEO. "I think he's an honorable guy," he said, according to the Times.

#5037. BMW is going upscale with its first 3-row EV, the iX7

BMW’s next electric SUV won’t just be bigger. The iX7 will sit at the top of the brand’s EV lineup when it debuts in 2027, bringing a more luxurious option to the mix.

After the new X5 and iX5, the X7, BMW’s biggest SUV, is due for a Neue Klasse overhaul. BMW said the second-generation X7 will arrive in 2027, including the first fully electric version, the iX7.

Ahead of its upcoming debut, camouflaged prototypes have been spotted testing, revealing a few design changes.

Although it won’t fully adopt BMW’s Neue Klasse design, like the iX3, the three-row SUV appears to feature a slimmer, more upright Kidney Grille instead of the traditional wide BMW Grille.

The iX7 also swaps conventional pull-style door handles for small handles on the vehicle’s pillar. It also retains split headlights rather than the updated Neue Klasse-style headlights on the iX3 (shown below on the right).

While BMW has yet to reveal details, a Reddit user who claims to work at BMW’s factory in South Carolina and has seen the second-generation X7 shared a few new details.

Like the X5 and iX5, the new X7 and iX7 will retain a more traditional design rather than going full-on Neue Klasse, including the Kidney Grille. The user also said BMW is dropping the split tailgate, which it did with the new X5, in favor of a standard one-piece tailgate.

Inside, the dashboard and center console are similar to the new X5, but with upgraded tech and features from the new 7 Series sedan.

BMW said the new 7 Series sedan “takes the lead when it comes to the introduction of technologies from the Neue Klasse into existing models.”

Optional features will reportedly include the upgraded 31.3″ 8K BMW Theatre Screen for rear passengers and a 14.6″ BMW Passenger Screen. It will also have optional touch screen panels on the rear doors, the user said.

While most of the claims make sense, we’ll have to wait for BMW to confirm it officially. The new X7 and iX7 are expected to debut in mid-2027.

Like the iX5 60 xDrive, the three-row iX7 is expected to use a dual-motor all-wheel-drive powertrain. The iX5’s AWD setup delivers a combined 578 hp (425 kW) and 593 lb-ft of torque, enabling it to hit 0 to 60 mph in 4.4 seconds.

The iX5 uses a massive 144 kWh battery, which BMW said offers up to 435 miles of range. It will also use an 800V electrical system for fast charging.

BMW showed just how fast it can charge during a media event in Southern California earlier this month, where the iX5 60 xDrive hit a peak of 464 kW. With up to 460 kW peak charging, the iX5 60 xDrive can add up to 170 miles of range in 10 minutes, BMW says.

The new X7 and iX7 are expected to go into production at BMW’s Spartanburg plant in SC in late 2027, joining the new iX5.

#5035. Ex-employee sues Ethereum Foundation and Vitalik Buterin over firing

Kayleen Fulton alleges she was fired less than two weeks after disclosing a cancer diagnosis and helping organize a staff letter challenging a requirement to endorse the foundation’s new mandate.

A former Ethereum Foundation employee has sued the organization, along with Ethereum co-founder Vitalik Buterin and four others.

Kayleen Fulton alleges she was fired after disclosing a cancer diagnosis and organizing a staff letter challenging a directive tied to the foundation’s new mandate.

Fulton, who led the foundation’s protocol support team and is known in the Ethereum community as “Nixo,” filed the complaint on Nov. 4 in the United States District Court for the District of Colorado. She alleges that Bastian Aue, a co-executive director at the time, fired her on April 6, 13 days after she disclosed her diagnosis. The complaint also alleges Buterin helped adopt and enforce the directive as a board member.

Fulton said she and colleague Trent Van Epps organized a letter in response to the mandate with 53 signers. It backed the mandate’s goals while objecting to a requirement that employees affirm it to keep their jobs. The complaint alleges that days later, on April 6, Aue fired Fulton and Van Epps 30 minutes apart. Fulton alleges disability discrimination, retaliation and interference with her medical leave rights and is seeking lost pay and damages.

The complaint also says some employees objected to a reference to ritual suicide in the mandate and quotes Aue as telling staff that the language was figurative.

In a May 14 letter quoted in the complaint, a lawyer for the foundation’s Singapore affiliate, Ethereum Asia Pacific Limited, denied that Fulton had been subjected to unlawful conduct or retaliation and said her separation agreement was binding. Cointelegraph reached out to the foundation, Fulton and Van Epps for comment, but had not received a response by publication.

#5033. Binance warns iOS flaw could expose crypto wallet keys

The exchange urged iPhone users to remove apps installed from untrusted sources, and it is unclear whether Apple’s latest iOS updates fix the flaw.

Update (Nov. 10, 2026, 12:14 pm UTC): This article has been updated to include a response from a Binance spokesperson.

Cryptocurrency exchange Binance warned Tuesday that an iOS flaw could expose crypto wallet recovery phrases and private keys if a user runs a malicious app on an affected iPhone.

Binance listed iOS 26.7.1, 27.0.1 and 27.2 among the affected versions that could allow malicious apps to read local files from other apps, putting seed phrases, private keys and login credentials “at risk across the entire ecosystem.” Binance added that this was “an iOS-level issue, not unique to Binance.”

Binance urged users to delete any apps installed from untrusted sources, update iOS and all apps to the latest official versions, and transfer funds to new wallets created on clean devices if they suspect a breach already occurred.

Blockchain security firm SlowMist flagged the issue earlier on Tuesday, saying a malicious app could read recovery phrases if a wallet stores them unencrypted on the device.

An iOS security researcher who goes by forcequitOS publicly disclosed the flaw on Sunday and credited another researcher who goes by 0xjohnny.

Apple released iOS 27.2.1 and 26.7.3 on Monday, but it remains unclear whether either update fixes the flaw. Cointelegraph contacted Apple about the vulnerability, but had not received a response by publication.

A spokesperson for Binance told Cointelegraph that the “underlying file-access risk may apply across the iOS ecosystem” but said it identified no confirmed cases where attackers used this bug to access cryptocurrency wallets or steal funds.

#5032. Binance Halts MEXC Transfers as FinCEN Cuts Exchange Off From US Financial System

Binance Halts MEXC Transfers as FinCEN Cuts Exchange Off From US Financial System

Binance has stopped processing transactions involving MEXC, one of the world's largest crypto exchanges, days after the U.S. Treasury Department designated MEXC a primary money laundering concern.

"Effective immediately, users should not send funds to, receive funds from, or otherwise transact through Binance involving MEXC, MEXC Global, MEXC affiliates or related entities," Binance tweeted on Tuesday.

Binance is applying enhanced compliance measures following FinCEN’s designation of MEXC as a Primary Money Laundering Concern.

Effective immediately, users should not send funds to, receive funds from, or otherwise transact through Binance involving MEXC, MEXC Global, MEXC…

Any request to send funds from a Binance account to MEXC will be declined, according to a Binance announcement that lists 13 entities covered by the restriction, including MEXC Global Limited, MEXC Estonia OÜ and MEXC Singapore Pte Ltd. Deposits received from those entities will be temporarily withheld for compliance review, and users could face further restrictions, including being offboarded from Binance, the exchange said.

MEXC ranks ninth among spot exchanges on CoinMarketCap, which scores platforms on traffic, liquidity and trading volume, with about $812.5 million in spot volume over the past 24 hours. Binance ranks first, with about $6.4 billion.

The Treasury's Financial Crimes Enforcement Network (FinCEN) named MEXC a primary money laundering concern last Friday, in connection with Russian illicit finance. The order, issued under the Combating Russian Money Laundering Act, bars U.S. financial institutions from processing fund transfers involving the exchange.

FinCEN alleges that MEXC processed at least $16 billion in suspicious transactions between May 2021 and May 2026, including nearly $1.5 billion in direct and indirect exposure to Russian sanctions evasion, and described its anti-money laundering controls as severely deficient.

The order takes effect 30 days after its publication in the Federal Register.

Binance is not the only exchange to respond. OKX has also warned its users against transferring funds to MEXC, Finance Magnates reported.

#5030. Coinbase, Kalshi join lawsuit backing CFTC over US stock perp futures

Coinbase, Kalshi and four other companies backed the CFTC in a lawsuit over whether US exchanges can list perpetual futures tied to individual stocks.

Coinbase, Kalshi and four other companies asked a federal court Monday to accept a brief backing the United States Commodity Futures Trading Commission (CFTC) in a lawsuit over perpetual futures tied to individual US stocks.

According to a proposed amicus brief shared Monday by Coinbase legal representative Molly Abraham, the group also includes Kraken parent Payward, trading companies DRW Holdings and Jump Trading, and crypto investment company Paradigm Operations. The companies argue that stock perpetual futures fall under existing US futures laws.

The filing comes days after a federal judge found that the CFTC likely “exceeded its statutory authority” in allowing single-stock perpetuals to trade as security futures.

The case could determine whether US exchanges can offer retail traders contracts that track individual stocks without expiring.

On Nov. 3, the CFTC announced that single-stock perpetual futures, or stock perps, could begin trading on US-regulated exchanges for the first time.

The Capital Markets Alliance Association (CMAA), a newly formed trade and advocacy group representing traditional financial market participants and whose members include Cboe and Citadel Securities, sued the CFTC last week, challenging the CFTC’s authority to allow the contracts to trade.

Stock perps allow traders to speculate on the price movements of individual stocks without owning the shares. Unlike traditional futures, they have no fixed expiration date.

The CMAA argues that because the contracts lack a fixed expiration date, they should be regulated as security-based swaps, a type of derivative linked to stocks or other securities that is primarily overseen by the Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC) and faces tighter restrictions on retail trading.

The six companies backing the CFTC disagree, arguing that a fixed expiration date is not required for a contract to qualify as a security future, which falls under joint CFTC and SEC oversight.

Related: Coinbase’s US national trust bank charter takes effect

Following the CMAA’s complaint, US District Judge Valerie Caproni said the plaintiff was likely to succeed in its claim that the CFTC exceeded its statutory authority by allowing stock perps to trade as security futures.

Caproni found that the contracts appeared to qualify as swaps rather than security futures, casting doubt on the agency’s legal interpretation.

However, she denied CMAA’s request for a temporary restraining order, finding that the group had failed to demonstrate imminent, irreparable harm. The decision left the CFTC’s trading approvals in place while the lawsuit proceeds.

CMAA is scheduled to file its reply to the CFTC’s opposition to a preliminary injunction on Tuesday.

#5027. Waymo adds nearly 250 square miles of robotaxi coverage in 5 cities

Waymo is growing its driverless service area by nearly 250 square miles over the next week, spread across Los Angeles, Nashville, Orlando, San Diego, and the San Francisco Bay Area.

That’s about as big as Tesla’s entire Houston Robotaxi geofence after this week’s expansion, and Waymo is adding it in cities where it already runs with nobody in the driver’s seat.

The company announced the expansion in a short post on its blog today. Here’s what changes in each market, with Waymo’s updated service area maps:

Service now runs down the coast into the South Bay, through Manhattan Beach and Redondo Beach to Torrance.

The zone pushes further south, and riders can now get to the zoo.

Coverage on the east side nearly doubles, and there’s more service downtown.

The service area now stretches north to La Jolla, taking in University City and Clairemont along the way.

Waymo is pushing further into the South Bay, including a connection to BART at Berryessa station in San Jose. The service area now runs from San Francisco down the Peninsula to San Jose and Campbell.

Waymo didn’t break out square mileage by city, and it didn’t say how many vehicles it’s adding to cover the new ground. The updated maps are already in the Waymo app.

Some of these markets are very young. Waymo opened Nashville to public riders in April with a zone of about 60 square miles covering downtown, Midtown, Music Row, and East Nashville. San Diego only went public on September 1, with roughly 50 square miles between the beach and I-15 that barely crossed north of I-8. So getting to La Jolla less than two months later is a big jump.

In May, Waymo pushed its coverage past 1,400 square miles across 11 cities, which is bigger than the state of Rhode Island. Since then, it went driverless in Las Vegas, launched San Diego, and now says it operates in 15 US metros with more than 500,000 paid rides per week. It’s even mapping Toronto for a 2028 launch.

Tesla is also drawing bigger maps. After launching Robotaxi in Dallas and Houston with tiny geofences in April, it stretched Houston to about 254 square miles this week, according to Robotaxi Tracker. But the tracker counts 34 vehicles in Houston.

I'd like to see Waymo add more airports to their service list. Airports are huge drivers of both ride count and revenue for ride services. Waymo currently serves airports in some of their cities, such as Phoenix, San Francisco, and Nashville. However, other major airports such as LAX, Atlanta, and Dallas/Fort Worth do not currently have terminal service even though Waymo operates in those cities. Adding these might not look flashy on a map, but it would be a huge addition to Waymo's bottom line.

And on the metric that reflects actual use, it’s not close. Waymo logged 3.9 million rider-only miles per week in Q2 2026, while Tesla’s driverless fleet did 558,000 miles in its latest reported week, mostly in Austin. That’s about 7x, and it comes on the same day Elon Musk claimed the robotaxi race against Waymo “was never a real contest.”

I like this. Good progress. But I’ll be truly impressed when the Bay Area service area extends south into LA, expands north, and the same happens in San Diego.

It’s going to happen. Waymo is already dabbling with highway operations in California.I can see them linking all those service areas within the next 2 years. Maybe even by the end of next year if all goes well.

#5026. Binance Warns of Apple iOS Flaw That Could Expose iPhone Users' Private Keys

Binance Warns of Apple iOS Flaw That Could Expose iPhone Users' Private Keys

Binance has warned iPhone users about a vulnerability in Apple's iOS that it says could let malicious apps read files stored by other apps, putting crypto wallet seed phrases, private keys and login credentials at risk.

The flaw, dubbed "worse_query," affects iOS 26.7.1, 27.0.1 and 27.2, the exchange tweeted on Tuesday. "This is an iOS-level issue, not unique to Binance," it said.

A vulnerability (worse_query) affecting iOS 26.7.1, 27.0.1, and 27.2 allows malicious apps to read local files from other apps, putting seed phrases, private keys, and login credentials at risk across the entire ecosystem. This is an iOS-level issue, not unique to Binance.…

Binance urged users to delete any apps installed from untrusted sources, such as sideloaded or manually signed app files, and to update iOS and all apps to the latest official versions. Anyone who suspects a breach should move their funds to a new wallet created on a clean device and reset all active login sessions, it said.

iPhone apps normally run inside a sandbox, which stops them from accessing other apps' private files. A researcher who goes by forcequitOS published worse_query on GitHub as a sandbox escape for iOS 26.0 through 26.7.2 and iOS 27.2, describing it as a proof of concept for developers that "isn't useful by itself." Among the locations it can reach is the folder where iOS apps store their data, according to the repository, which calls it the "direct successor" to an earlier exploit, bad_query. There have been no confirmed reports of crypto being stolen using the flaw.

Apple released iOS 27.2.1 on Monday, a day after the proof of concept was published. Its security notes list fixes for nine vulnerabilities, including an authorization issue in App Intents through which "an app may be able to access sensitive user data," as well as several Bluetooth and iMessage flaws.

However, the notes do not mention worse_query or its researcher, and Apple has not publicly confirmed whether the update addresses the flaw Binance described. At least one security expert believes it does. 23pds, chief information security officer at blockchain security firm SlowMist, urged users to update to iOS 27.2.1, saying in a tweet that the update would block the worse_query sandbox escape, along with other threats.

It is not the first time attackers have targeted Apple devices. Earlier this year, Google warned of iOS malware that targeted crypto apps on unpatched devices, while in August, the Dutch national cyber agency said hackers were exploiting a flaw in Apple's macOS Screen Sharing feature to plant Monero miners on Macs.

#5025. Rivian electric van spotted in Walmart+ livery, hinting at a fleet deal

A Rivian electric delivery van wrapped in Walmart+ branding was photographed on a car hauler on I-94 in northwest Indiana today. It’s the first public sign that Walmart could become Rivian’s next big van customer after Amazon.

Neither company has announced a deal. But Walmart’s last two electric van suppliers are out of the business, and Rivian is one of the few companies still building a purpose-built electric delivery van in the US.

Reddit user biggie_ballzzz posted the photo to r/RIVNstock this afternoon, asking if anyone had heard about a partnership. The van is strapped to a multi-car trailer between two Ford pickups, with a big “Walmart+” logo on the side and “Same day. Right to your door.” on the rear doors.

There’s no mistaking the vehicle. It’s Rivian’s Commercial Van, with the same tall body and red light bar wrapping around the rear that you see on Amazon Prime vans everywhere.

A few commenters suspected an AI image, but there’s no clear evidence. In fact, a second commenter then shared a photo of a van in the same livery parked in front of a Walmart Supercenter in Hammond, Indiana, about 30 miles to the west.

Rivian (RIVN) stock was up more than 3% this afternoon as the photo made the rounds, according to screenshots shared in the thread. That’s two days before Rivian reports its Q3 2026 results on Thursday, October 29.

Walmart has been trying to electrify last-mile delivery for years. Its supplier picks haven’t aged well.

In 2022, it signed up to buy 4,500 Canoo vans and put them to work on InHome deliveries. Canoo filed for Chapter 7 bankruptcy in January 2025 and ceased operations.

The same year, Walmart reserved 5,000 BrightDrop vans from GM, and officially added them to its delivery fleet in late 2024. Then GM killed BrightDrop production at its CAMI plant in Ontario a year ago, with no plan to build the van anywhere else. BrightDrop was on pace for roughly 4,000 US sales in 2025, compared to more than 60,000 for GM’s gas-powered Express and Savana vans.

So Walmart is running two orphaned EV van platforms. And if it wants more purpose-built electric vans at scale, the list of US options is very short.

Amazon ordered 100,000 vans from Rivian, and its fleet has now passed 40,000 units on the road. But the deal came with an exclusivity period that ended in November 2023, and Rivian has struggled to land anything close to Amazon-scale since.

AT&T signed up for a pilot in late 2023, and HelloFresh came on board in 2025. In February 2025, Rivian opened van sales to any business, with the RCV 500 starting at $79,900 and the larger RCV 700 at $83,900. None of those customers moves the needle like Walmart would.

There’s also a charging angle. Walmart now operates its own 400 kW fast-charging network at 100 stores in 20 states, and its manager of retail charging, Wade Higgins, previously worked at Rivian, according to his bio on the board of Minnesota car-sharing nonprofit HOURCAR.

One van on a trailer isn’t a fleet order. Not yet, anyway. This could be a test unit for a pilot program, like the one AT&T announced in 2023. And since Rivian sells vans to any business now, Walmart could have simply bought a few to try them out.

So Walmart is running two orphaned EV van platforms. And if it wants more purpose-built electric vans at scale, the list of US options is very short.

Walmart also runs Ford E-Transit vans but I can't find recent numbers for how many

But the timing makes sense. Walmart bet on Canoo and GM, and both left it with vans nobody builds anymore. If you’re Walmart and you want to keep electrifying same-day delivery, Rivian is the obvious call. Its plant in Normal, Illinois, has already built tens of thousands of these vans, and the platform has been proven at scale by the one company Walmart is trying to beat on delivery.

That’s the awkward part. Walmart putting its brand on the same van as Amazon Prime is a bit funny, and it says a lot about how thin the electric van market has gotten in the US.

For Rivian, a Walmart deal would matter. The van business gives it volume in Normal while R2 ramps, and it badly needs proof that the van isn’t just an Amazon product. We’ll see if Rivian says anything on Thursday’s call. We wouldn’t bet on a massive order announcement, but a Walmart pilot wouldn’t surprise us one bit.

#5024. Billy Joel at Home: 10 Photos of the Piano Man Through the Decades

For Billy Joel, New York is more than just a place. It’s an inextricable part of what makes the singer who he is. “My speech patterns are New York. The music that I like, it was all New York–based. That’s what I think of when I think of home,” he said in his 2025 HBO documentary Billy Joel: And So It Goes. “It’s my roots.”

The Bronx native grew up in Hicksville, a middle-class suburb of Nassau County on Long Island. His family struggled to make ends meet. “There was poor, and then there was Billy Joel poor. He was lucky if he had enough money to buy a pack of cigarettes,” his childhood friend and fellow musician Jon Small said in And So It Goes. According to Joel’s sister Judy, no matter how hard times were, their mother Rosalind insisted he take piano lessons. His talent “had to be nurtured,” Judy said in the HBO doc. “She knew that he was going to be something someday.”

Before he indeed became something, Joel worked on an oyster boat. “I used to look up at this mansion on the hill and wonder what it would be like to live in a house like that,” he said in the documentary, referencing the waterfront property on Centre Island he nicknamed MiddleSea. “I used to think, ‘They’re rich bastards. They never had to work a day in their life.’ Well, I own that house now. It’s not finished yet. And neither am I.”

Read on for a glimpse into the Grammy winner’s life, from his early days in New York to his brief stint in Los Angeles and return home.

Joel’s music career took the Long Island native to Los Angeles in the early 1970s, and he signed with Columbia Records in 1973. After three years in LA, he and his first wife, Elizabeth Weber, returned to his home state, where he completed his fourth studio album, Turnstiles. “I wanted to get back to New York and make more of a rock and roll album,” Joel explained in And So It Goes. “I was on a bus on my way back to Highland Falls, New York. Elizabeth found us a house up on the Hudson River, and it was fall. It was just gorgeous. The colors were ablaze, the trees were, like, gold and red, and I remember thinking, ‘This is the New York State that I loved when I was a kid. This is magic. We don’t have this in California.’ And I wrote this love song to New York on the bus.” It would become “New York State of Mind,” one of Joel’s most popular tunes.

The singer-songwriter poses with some of his records in January 1978.

Joel’s fifth studio album, 1977’s The Stranger, was a runaway success—but the singer, pictured alongside his accolades in New York in 1978, told Playboy a few years later that his newfound fortune didn’t faze him. “I read these stories about Peter Frampton’s multimillion-dollar mansion, 15 Rolls-Royces and all that,” he said. “Who cares? It’s all bullshit. What was his last record like? Was it any good? That’s the question. People get hung up on the money thing. It’s nice to have four motorcycles. It’s fun. And I walk into the garage and go, ‘Holy shit! I’ve got four motorcycles!’ But I don’t go beyond a certain point.”

Joel, his then-wife Elizabeth, and their dog in 1978.

Instead of splurging on a million-dollar mansion, Joel told Playboy that when he and his wife decided to buy a home, “We got a $300,000 house with a mortgage like everybody else.” Their glass house, featuring floor-to-ceiling windows overlooking the Long Island Sound, was located in the town of Oyster Bay on Long Island’s North Shore. Joel appears poised to hurl a rock at the home on the cover of his 1980 album, Glass Houses. “The old thing about people who live in glass houses shouldn’t throw stones—I don’t believe it,” he said. “I think, Why not? Take chances. I really wanted to throw a rock at my own house.”

Joel and Elizabeth at their Long Island residence.

In the 1970s, Joel asked Weber to be his manager. “The company I’m with now is New York–based,” Joel said of his representation. “It’s called Home Run—nothing to do with baseball. It’s a home-run agency.” Weber got right to work. She recalled in his HBO documentary that the “story [Joel] likes to tell” of her early days on the job is that he woke up the next morning to phones everywhere and “cabinets installed.” However, she said, “That’s not really true, but it’s close to true.” The couple (seen here at Joel’s grand piano in their living room) split time between their Manhattan apartment, where they worked, and Oyster Bay. Weber left her role managing Joel when the two split, and her brother Frank picked up where she left off. Home Run dissolved amid financial woes in the ’80s, and Joel eventually pressed charges against Frank over mishandled funds; the parties reached a settlement, the terms of which have not been disclosed publicly.

The musician mixing tapes in the music studio of his New York home.

The Oyster Bay house, a midcentury-modern residence, was not far from where Joel grew up in Hicksville. Photographed here in his at-home music studio in 1978, the singer’s career was on the upswing—but his relationship with Weber was growing tense. “Having a big boom in record success, I think my marriage was starting to unravel around then; 1979, it wasn’t the happiest time,” Joel once said. “Record success doesn’t translate into happiness.” Joel assumed things were paying off financially, but he admitted he “wasn’t aware” how much money he was actually bringing in. “I know we were living in a nice house, my wife was going out and buying all kinds of nice stuff,” he said. “I never really went out and did that.”

Joel multitasks in his LA home studio in 1984.

Though Joel had settled down on the East Coast, he told the Los Angeles Times that his California years cross his mind “all the time.” The singer, pictured here in LA in 1984, wrote his hit song “Piano Man” based on his experience as a lounge musician (using the name Bill Martin) at a now-defunct bar near the corner of Wilshire Boulevard and Western Avenue during the early 1970s. “I wonder what happened to the people I knew—the people in the bar in ‘Piano Man,’” he said. “It was the Executive Room in the Wilshire district. I got tips and made union scale, and I was able to afford an apartment in Studio City. The weather was great; the people were nice.”

Though it appears Joel lived like a rock star in Los Angeles—as seen here in 1984, perched on a sheepskin-style throw over a leather sofa with a pack of Marlboros and a landline telephone by his side—he told the Los Angeles Times in 2023 that he felt more like “an exiled writer living in Paris” during his early stint in the City of Angels. “I went from being an insignificant nobody to the Piano Man while I was there,” he said. “I still look back to my time in LA with a certain amount of romance.”

Joel is pictured here at the dinner table in 2001. When The New Yorker paid him a visit at his Centre Island residence in 2014, he described the interior’s aesthetic as “Catholic martyrs and saints, grotesqueries.” In the library, Joel—who decorated the place himself—had a model of the Queen Mary on his mantel, and in the living room facing the fireplace was a triptych of the Last Supper along with silver jugs and bowls on a nearby table.

Joel’s various properties have passed through some famous hands. He and his second wife, supermodel Christie Brinkley, owned a 12-acre oceanfront property in the East Hampton hamlet of Amagansett. The couple raised daughter Alexa Ray Joel in the Long Island dwelling. It was later sold to Jerry and Jessica Seinfeld for $32 million. According to The New Yorker, Joel also sold a duplex on Central Park West to singer-songwriter Sting for $5 million and a house on Martha’s Vineyard to businessman Mickey Drexler. At one point, Joel purchased a home in the Hamptons from Jaws star Roy Scheider.

“As a little kid, I always liked boats and the romance of living on an island,” Joel told Parade in 2018. The singer—pictured here on an East Hampton dock in 2001—has always felt at home on the water. In 2007 he designed a 57-foot powerboat called the Vendetta that can go about 50 knots, per The New Yorker. Boating represented “freedom,” Joel said in his documentary. “I am the captain of my ship.”

#5022. BYD’s $10,000 EV blows a tire at nearly 90 mph — Watch what happens

BYD updated its lowest-priced electric car, the Seagull, which starts at around $10,000 in China, with a few extras, including a high-speed tire blowout system that stabilizes the vehicle at nearly 90 mph.

If the $10,000 price tag wasn’t enough, BYD is giving buyers even more reason to check out its entry-level electric hatch.

The Seagull is among BYD’s most popular vehicles, sold as the Dolphin Surf in Europe and the UK and as the Dolphin Mini in other global markets.

BYD launched the second-generation Seagull EV last week, starting at just 62,900 yuan, or about $9,500. That’s including BYD’s limited-time Super Trade-In incentive.

Regular prices start at 65,900 yuan ($9,900), which is still less than its predecessor. The first-generation Seagull EV started at 69,900 yuan ($10,500) without incentives, making it BYD’s most affordable purely electric vehicle in China.

For its second generation, the Seagull is bigger, more advanced, and adds a bit more luxury to the entry-level segment.

The new BYD Seagull is 4,205 mm long, 1,810 mm wide, and 1,570 mm high, with a 2,650 mm wheelbase. That’s 425 mm longer and has a 150 mm longer wheelbase than the previous model.

Thanks to the added space, BYD’s electric hatch now offers room for five passengers, up from four seats that the first-gen Seagull had.

BYD added several new features with its latest tech, including a bigger 15.6″ infotainment screen with its DiLink 150 smart cockpit system and a “140 km/h high-speed tire blowout stability system” as standard.

To showcase how the tire blowout system works, BYD put the updated electric hatch to the test at full speed.

BYD’s general manager of the Ocean Series, Zhang Zhuo, shared a video of the second-generation Seagull EV handling the blowout at 140 km/h, or about 87 mph, like it’s nothing.

The high-speed tire-blowout system lets the Seagull stay in its lane until it comes to a complete stop. Pretty impressive for an entry-level vehicle.

As you can see in the video, the new version also has an available roof-mounted LiDAR. Buyers can upgrade to the optional DiPilot 300 system with BYD’s God’s Eye intelligent driving assistance. The ADAS system provides features like City Navigation on Autopilot (CNOA) and smart parking.

BYD added a few premium extras, including heating, ventilation, and a massage function to the front seats, plus ambient lighting, smart storage options, and a 50W wireless phone charger.

The new Seagull uses BYD’s Blade Batteries 2.0 and Flash Charging Technology. As such, the battery can charge from 10% to 70% in five minutes. A full recharge, or 10% to 97%, takes just nine minutes. Even in temperatures as low as -30°C, it takes 12 minutes to charge from 20% to 97%.

It’s available with two LFP battery packs: 30.1 kWh and 39.2 kWh, providing CLTC ranges of 320 km (199 miles) and 420 km (261 miles), respectively.

To improve the ride quality and handling, BYD upgraded the rear suspension to a multi-link independent system.

BYD is leaving the US so far behind in technology, it obviously it will never catch up as a result of it’s backwards MAGA leadership.

The second-gen Seagull also switches to front-wheel-drive (FWD) with a more powerful 85 kW (114 hp) or 95 kW (127 hp) electric motor, up from the 55 kW (75 hp) that its predecessor offered.

BYD’s new Seagull is available in five variants, priced from 65,900 yuan to 99,900 yuan ($9,900 to $15,000), without a trade-in.

After launching the second-gen Seagull in China, BYD is expected to introduce the updated version overseas. It will likely be the second-generation Dolphin Surf and Dolphin Mini.

#5021. Bitcoin may benefit as global tightening slows, Binance Research says

Binance Research says Bitcoin could find support as global monetary tightening slows, even as markets price in a December Fed rate hike.

Bitcoin could find support before major central banks start cutting rates if the spread of rate hikes continues to slow, Binance Research said Tuesday.

Eight of the 10 central banks Binance tracks had raised rates over the last six months, including Sweden’s announced increase on Nov. 4, with the momentum of tightening starting to ease since its July peak, Binance Research said in a Tuesday report shared with Cointelegraph.

“Whether this easing continues into early 2027 will be key to judging whether the macro backdrop is turning less hostile for crypto,” the report said.

Binance pointed to the previous cycle. Bitcoin (BTC) bottomed in November 2022, and its six-month return turned positive in February 2023, before any of the central banks in the index began cutting rates.

Pace of G10 banks tightening and Bitcoin’s 6-month returns. Source: Binance Research

Similarly, in 2024, monetary tightening measures led to Bitcoin delivering its “strongest” six-month return of the cycle, exceeding 160% in March, Binance said.

“In each case, BTC moved with the direction of global policy, not its level,” the report adds.

Related: Analysts see no broader ‘contagion’ from $6.3B Binance hack

A US rate increase remains in play. Federal Reserve Governor Christopher Waller said Tuesday he was leaning toward a quarter-point hike in December and would consider a half-point move if core inflation remained elevated. Odds of a 25-basis-point hike in December rose from 76.3% to 96.3% on Tuesday, according to CME Group’s FedWatch Tool.

In a Nov. 2 report, Bitwise also argued that a slowdown in economic growth expectations could eventually prompt central banks to pivot towards easing monetary policy, potentially creating a more favourable environment for Bitcoin.

BTC/USD, 1-year chart. Source: CoinMarketCap

Bitcoin’s price fell 3.5% during the past week, extending its one-year decline to 30%. The world’s first cryptocurrency was trading at $74,131, or 41% below its $126,198 all-time high, according to CoinMarketCap.

#5020. Morning Minute: Vitalik Recasts ETH as a Store of Value

Morning Minute: Vitalik Recasts ETH as a Store of Value

Morning Minute is a daily newsletter written by Tyler Warner. The analysis and opinions expressed are his own and do not necessarily reflect those of Decrypt. And check out our daily news show 'FOMO HOUR' covering all of the top stories and market action.

🏛️ Vitalik Recasts ETH as a Store of Value

Vitalik Buterin spent Sunday on X telling Ethereum holders to stop valuing ETH as a fee-burning token and start valuing it as a store of value. “We should not feel sad if fee burn is less than 10% of breakeven indefinitely,” he wrote. “The value of ETH comes from elsewhere.”

That elsewhere is stability, which he ties to decentralization, censorship resistance and security. It replaces the ultrasound money pitch, the idea that network usage burns supply faster than staking creates it. The burn has not kept up in years. Through the first nine months of 2026 it offset about 2% of issuance while supply grew 0.64%, mostly because Ethereum’s own rollup roadmap moved activity and fees off the mainnet.

Buterin’s argument is that this is fine, and that chasing the burn would be worse. Maximizing fee revenue means wanting congestion and expensive blockspace. He warned it would turn Ethereum into “a world-scale major corporation” at the cost of what makes it worth using. The burn, he added, was “never a true floor” and came mostly from internal crypto churn like lending and “silly NFT purchases.”

Supporters are already building out the new frame. Ethlabs co-founder Binji Pande calls ETH a future-proof store of value and argues it beats Bitcoin on quantum and AI risk, citing eleven years of trading history and an Ethereum Foundation that holds under 0.2% of supply. Julian Ma of Ethlabs makes the related case that Ethereum’s security culture positions it as a programmable store of value as AI pressures cryptography.

#5019. How Big of a Calorie Deficit Do You Need to Achieve Body Recomp?

Your calorie deficit could be working against you. Here’s how to find the right balance.

With body recomposition come two goals: lose body fat while gaining muscle. At first glance, it might seem a bit too good to be true, if you think about how each goal is achieved individually. But it’s not.

To lose body fat, you need to eat fewer calories than you burn in a day. To gain muscle, you need to continuously challenge your muscles in the gym, and eat enough to hit your protein goals, which is vital to building muscle mass—especially in a calorie deficit.

With the two goals seemingly at nutritional odds, it’s difficult to determine exactly how you should eat to hit this two-for-one goal. Exactly how big of a calorie deficit do you need to achieve body recomp? We tapped an expert to find out.

Get your personalized plan with our Eating for Body Recomp guide

Achieving body recomp takes some strategic nutritional planning, starting with a balanced calorie goal.

Keep your calorie deficit on the more conservative side when it comes to body recomp so you have enough energy to power your important workouts, Dana White, RD, previously told WH. Aim for a calorie deficit no larger than 250 to 500 calories per day. Any larger than that, and you run the risk of feeling under-fueled in the gym or not hitting your protein goals—which is major for building and maintaining muscle mass.

With body recomp, though, it’s important to get even more granular and understand what’s making up those cals, says White. “It’s not just about eating calories—it’s the type of calories.”

One of the best ways to determine how to fill those calories is by tracking your macronutrients—carbohydrates, proteins, and fats. To find out how much of each macro you should get, check out our Eating for Body Recomp guide.

Cori Ritchey, C.S.C.S., is the fitness editor at Women's Health, as well as a certified strength and condition coach and group fitness instructor. She’s reported on topics regarding health, nutrition, mental health, fitness, sex, and relationships for several years. You can find more of her work in Men’s Health, HealthCentral, Livestrong, Self, and others.

#5017. Moody’s Taps Chainlink to Send Credit Ratings Across Multiple Blockchains

Moody’s Taps Chainlink to Send Credit Ratings Across Multiple Blockchains

Chainlink and Moody’s Ratings have completed a pilot showing how credit ratings could be beamed across multiple blockchains at once, another sign that the traditional machinery of finance is steadily moving onchain.

Announced Tuesday, the test used Chainlink’s Cross-Chain Interoperability Protocol, or CCIP, to distribute Moody’s ratings simultaneously across both permissioned and permissionless blockchain networks.

It ran through the Moody’s Ratings Token Integration Engine, or TIE, the system the 125-year-old ratings giant built to disseminate credit assessments and related data within blockchain environments. The pilot, the companies said, preserved Moody’s existing publication and governance processes while proving the ratings could travel across chains through a single interoperable layer.

The logic is straightforward: as more bonds, funds, and other assets are issued as blockchain tokens, the investors trading them will expect the credit intelligence they rely on to be available wherever those assets live, rather than stopping at any one network’s edge.

Moody’s brings real experience here, having rated or assessed more than 70 digital issuances, including digital bonds and tokenized funds, between January 2017 and October 2026.

“As more financial assets move onchain, credit intelligence has to move with them, across chains and through every rating change,” said Johann Eid, chief business officer at Chainlink Labs, calling the pilot a foundational piece of what institutional capital needs to adopt tokenization at scale.

Fabian Astic, Moody’s global head of digital economy, argued that “the future of tokenized finance is unlikely to reside on a single blockchain, platform, or market infrastructure,” and that investors will increasingly expect ratings regardless of the underlying technology.

The effort builds on Moody’s steady push onchain. Earlier this year, the firm launched TIE on the institutional Canton Network before extending it to Solana, making its ratings machine-readable on a major public blockchain.

Chainlink, meanwhile, has positioned CCIP as the connective tissue for institutional tokenization, recently rolling out an upgraded version and reporting tens of billions of dollars in cross-chain value secured as banks and asset managers experiment with moving assets between networks.

The pilot, though, ran entirely on a testnet using representative test data, with no actual rating actions published onchain and no live dissemination involved.

It demonstrated technical capability rather than a launched product, but the companies said the model is designed to accommodate future rating updates under appropriate governance. For now, it’s a proof of concept, albeit one pointing toward a future where even credit ratings are just another piece of data flowing across blockchains.

#5015. Renewables to reach 40% of total generating capacity, beat gas within a year – EIA

Over the coming year, renewables’ share of total generating capacity will grow from 37.7% to 40.4%, surpassing that of natural gas and outstripping nuclear capacity fivefold, according to new data released by the US Energy Information Administration (EIA) and reviewed by the SUN DAY Campaign.

According to the EIA’s latest “Electric Power Monthly” report (with data through August 31, 2026), renewably generated electricity during the first two-thirds of 2026 was 10.2% greater year over year. The growth was led by utility-scale [>1 MW] solar (up 20.0%), small-scale solar (up 12.0%), wind (up 7.8%), hydropower (up 4.2%), and geothermal (up 3.8%).

From January to August 2026, wind produced 333,367 gigawatt-hours (GWh) – 10.6% of total US electrical generation – while utility-scale and small-scale solar combined produced 329,119 GWh (10.5%), hydropower produced 180,940 GWh (5.7%), biomass produced 29,917 GWh (1.0%), and geothermal produced 10,766 GWh (0.34%).

By comparison, the electrical output of US natural gas and nuclear power plants grew more slowly – by just 2.6% and 1.3%, respectively. Electricity produced by US coal plants fell by 8.7%.

During the first eight months of 2026, the combination of wind and solar, including small-scale solar, accounted for almost 21.1% of domestic electricity production, with wind at 10.6% and solar at nearly 10.5%.

During the first two-thirds of this year, wind and solar combined produced 44.4% more electricity than coal and 24.8% more than nuclear.

Through the end of August, the mix of all renewables, including biomass, accounted for 28.1% of total US electrical generation – up from 26.2% a year earlier.

Between September 1, 2025, and August 31, 2026, the installed capacity of utility-scale solar increased by 27,141.7 MW, while that of small-scale solar and wind grew by 6,526.1 MW and 9,979.1 MW (including 800 MW of offshore wind), respectively. The combined capacity of all renewable energy sources, including hydropower, biomass, and geothermal, expanded by 43,497.9 MW.

In addition, utility-scale battery energy storage capacity increased by 17,586.9 MW.

By comparison, coal capacity fell by 2,703.4 MW, while nuclear capacity remained unchanged. However, natural gas capacity rose by 5,316.0 MW. Combined, net new nuclear and fossil fuel capacity totaled just 2,612.6 MW.

Thus, renewable energy’s capacity expansion was nearly 17 times greater than the net growth of fossil fuels and nuclear power combined.

The EIA projects that the rapid growth of renewables over the past year will accelerate in the coming year.

As of September 1, 2026, renewable energy’s share of total US utility-scale generating capacity was 34.5%. EIA projects this to grow to 37.3% by August 31, 2027. Utility-scale solar will add 44,603.1 MW, thereby expanding its share from 13.4% to 16.5%, while wind will grow by 8,312.3 MW (including 3,355.0 MW of offshore wind), increasing from 13.4% to 13.5%. The mix of other renewables (hydropower, biomass, and geothermal) will add 201.9 MW.

The combined net capacity growth of all utility-scale renewable energy sources over the 12-month period (53,117.3 MW) is almost 44% higher than that added over the previous 12 months (36,971.8 MW). It would bring renewables’ share of total US utility-scale generating capacity up to 37.3%.

Meanwhile, EIA projects no new nuclear generating capacity and a net decline of 1,997.0 MW in fossil fuel capacity.

The figures cited above don’t include small-scale solar. The estimated capacity of small-scale solar systems grew by 6,526.1 MW during the last year, bringing its total to 63,192.6 MW. EIA does not provide a forecast for small-scale solar capacity growth over the next 12 months, but the SUN DAY Campaign assumes it will roughly equal the 6,400 MW added on average over each of the past five years. Therefore, it is conservatively assumed that an additional 6,000 MW or more will come online in the coming year.

If small-scale solar increases by approximately 6,000 MW by September 1, 2027, it will bring renewable energy’s installed capacity to about 550,000 MW. By comparison, natural gas’ projected generating capacity would total 518,312.8 MW.

Solar power’s share alone would be more than one-fifth (20.7%) of total US generating capacity.

EIA foresees battery energy storage adding another 23,656.2 MW by September 1, 2027 – an increase of 43.7%, bringing the total up to 77,753.2 MW.

Thus, the combination of utility-scale renewable energy sources and battery energy storage will provide 76,773.5 MW of new clean energy capacity by late summer 2027. With the inclusion of small-scale solar, that figure could rise to 82.8 GW of new generating capacity.

Google CAISO to see what percent of the California grid is supplied by renewable energy on each and every day, typically more than 70% and often 100%., And they have 16 GW of battery storage, providing renewable electricity 24 seven to the California electrical grid.

“Each new issue of EIA’s ‘Electric Power Monthly’ reinforces the theme that the growth of solar, wind, and battery storage has become unstoppable,” noted the SUN DAY Campaign’s executive director Ken Bossong. “Meanwhile, the share of the nation’s electrical generation and generating capacity provided by fossil fuels and nuclear continues to shrink.”

#5014. Bitcoin’s ‘best buying opportunity ever’ has arrived: Mark Moss

Bitcoin Opportunity Fund co-founder Mark Moss says that with banks and ETFs widening access to Bitcoin he expects a new record high in 2027.

Wall Street’s embrace of Bitcoin has created the “best buying opportunity ever,” according to investor Mark Moss.

Moss co-founded the Bitcoin Opportunity Fund, where he is an advisory partner. In 2025, UK-listed Satsuma Technology appointed him chief Bitcoin strategist.

Moss tells Cointelegraph on the latest episode of Trade Secrets that he first bought Bitcoin in 2015 at about $300, during a recovery from a $1,000 to $200 plunge.

“It was so risky,” Moss recalls of buying back then. In contrast, today, “you have consensus from the US government and Wall Street.”

“But most people still think it’s a scam,” he says.

That skepticism opens buying opportunities as Bitcoin starts to cross what Moss calls the “chasm” between early adopters and mainstream investors. Banks and exchange-traded funds (ETFs) have made Bitcoin easier to buy and financial advisers could help persuade clients who have stayed away previously to consider investing.

Related: Strategy shareholders approve daily dividends for four preferred stocks

Bitcoin briefly climbed above $80,000 after the Democrats took control of the US Congress in the midterm elections, then gave back those gains as tech stocks sold off. By Friday, it fell to $74,762, its lowest level since August and remains in the doldrums at the time of writing.

Despite the selloff, Moss points to the reaction to the $6.3 billion Binance hack as a sign that sellers were running out.

Bitcoin fell to $76,320 after the breach and slid to $74,762 on Friday, erasing its post-election gains. It fell further to about $73,500 late Sunday, its lowest level since August, after Nvidia’s board announced it would investigate reports that CEO Jensen Huang had been targeted by a foreign intelligence effort.

Bitcoin found another bottom after reports of Nvidia CEO’s scandal. Source: CoinMarketCap

Flows into US spot Bitcoin ETFs swung sharply during election week. The funds took in about $75 million on Wednesday, then shed $430 million on Thursday.

History is on the side of hodlers however. Bitcoin gained an average of about 53% in the 12 months after previous US midterms, Moss says, using data going back to 2014. That leaves three completed election cycles in his sample. He says he expects Bitcoin to set a new all-time high in 2027.

Moss thinks the Bitcoin treasury company to own is Strategy (MSTR), but believes that holding Bitcoin yourself is still superior. Strategy began buying Bitcoin in 2020 and turned those purchases into a core part of its business. It has used stock and debt sales to fund its purchases, giving investors a way to bet on Bitcoin through a publicly traded company.

By Nov. 2, Strategy held 849,327 BTC, more than 4% of Bitcoin’s maximum supply.

“I don’t think there’s another publicly traded company that will outperform Strategy over the next ten years,” Moss says.

Strategy has the world’s largest corporate Bitcoin trove. Source: BitcoinTreasuries.Net

Leverage can help a Bitcoin treasury company’s shares outperform the cryptocurrency it holds when its price rises.

But even if Strategy outperforms, Moss wouldn’t trade his Bitcoin holdings for its shares.

Related: 1inch lawyer sees agencies driving US crypto regulation as midterms unfold

Before Moss bought his first Bitcoin, he made a living buying foreclosed properties, fixing them up and selling them. He moved into property development, then built and sold a medical equipment business. He says he put the proceeds into Southern California real estate.

When the market crashed in 2008, he lost it all.

“The reward of being right is not worth the risk of getting it wrong,” he says.

Even with his outlook for Strategy, Moss says he’d keep his Bitcoin and use only a small share of his portfolio to pursue higher returns through treasury stocks.

In Moss’s model, Bitcoin could reach $1 million under certain circumstances, when it competes with stocks, bonds, gold and real estate for a considerable share of investors’ wealth. The model estimates how much those assets are worth, projects how that pool could grow as the global money supply expands and calculates a Bitcoin price based on the share it captures.

Financial advisers could help bring in that money via one client’s portfolio at a time, encouraging them to shift a few percentage points out of stocks or bonds and into Bitcoin. The scenario depends on a critical mass of clients doing that, to the point where all those small allocations add up.

The model also assumes Bitcoin stays secure and decentralized. Asked what could derail the scenario, Moss pointed to a failure that compromised either feature.

Moss says $1 million is possible by 2030... though hodler’s might have to wait until 2032.

Hodler’s Digest: Vitalik ‘pivots’ ETH to store of value, crypto suffers after US midterms
